\mathbf{R}\preceq_{T}C_{[A]^{<\theta}}
, then
\theta\leq\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{R})
and
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{R})\leq|A|
.

(2)

If
C_{[A]^{<\theta}}\preceq_{T}\mathbf{R}
, then
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{R})\leq\theta
and
|A|\leq\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{R})
.

In
Section

5.3
,
to obtain
5.61
from
5.60
using the
submodel method
,
“
\mathbf{R}\cong_{T}C_{[A]^{<\theta}}
” does not work, but “
\mathbf{R}\cong_{T}[A]^{<\theta}
” does (see
[
GKMS22
]
).
The following fact gives a sufficient condition which implies
C_{[A]^{<\theta}}\cong_{T}[A]^{<\theta}
.

Fact 5.7

(
[
CM22
, Lemma 1.15]
)
.

If
A
is a set with
|A|^{<\theta}=|A|
, then
C_{[A]^{<\theta}}\cong_{T}[A]^{<\theta}
.

Fact 5.8

(
[
CM22
, Lemma 2.11]
)
.

Let
A
be a set of size
\geq\theta
. Every ccc poset forces
[A]^{<\theta}\cong_{T}[A]^{<\theta}\cap V
and
C_{[A]^{<\theta}}\cong_{T}C_{[A]^{<\theta}}\cap V
. Moreover, it forces
\mathfrak{x}([A]^{<\theta})=\mathfrak{x}^{V}([A]^{<\theta})
where
\mathfrak{x}
represents “
\add
”, “
\cov
”, “
\non
” or “
\cof
”.

The following fact will be used to keep several cardinal invariants of Cichoń’s diagram small through the forcing iteration we shall perform in
Section

5.3
.

Fact 5.9

(
[
Yam25
, Corollary 2.16, also Example 2.15]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{P}
be a finite support iteration of ccc posets of length
\gamma\geq\theta
.

(1)

Assume that each iterand is either:

•

of size
<\theta
,

•

a subalgebra of random forcing, or

•

\sigma
-centered.

Then,
\mathbb{P}
forces
C_{[\gamma]^{<\theta}}\preceq_{T}\mathcal{N}
,
in particular,
\add(\mathcal{N})\leq\theta
.

(2)

Assume that each iterand is either:

•

of size
<\theta
, or

•

\sigma
-centered.

Then,
\mathbb{P}
forces
C_{[\gamma]^{<\theta}}\preceq_{T}C_{\mathcal{N}}^{\bot}
,
in particular,
\cov(\mathcal{N})\leq\theta
.

(3)

Assume that each iterand is:

•

of size
<\theta
.

Then,
\mathbb{P}
forces
C_{[\gamma]^{<\theta}}\preceq_{T}C_{\mathcal{M}}
, in particular,
\non(\mathcal{M})\leq\theta
.

We introduce the relational systems for
\non(M_{\mathscr{I}})
,
\non(K_{\mathscr{I}})
and their duals:

Definition 5.10
.

For an ideal
\mathscr{I}
on a countable set
X
, we define the following relational systems:

•

\mathbf{M}_{\mathscr{I}}\coloneqq\langle X^{\omega},\mathscr{I}^{(X^{<\omega})},\triangleleft^{*}\rangle
, where
x\triangleleft^{*}\phi:\Leftrightarrow\forall^{\infty}n<\omega~x(n)\in\phi(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n)
.

•

\mathbf{K}_{\mathscr{I}}\coloneqq\langle X^{\omega},\mathscr{I}^{\omega},\in^{*}\rangle
, where
x\in^{*}\phi:\Leftrightarrow\forall^{\infty}n<\omega~x(n)\in\phi(n)
.

Note that
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{M}_{\mathscr{I}})=\non(M_{\mathscr{I}})
,
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{M}_{\mathscr{I}})=\cov(M_{\mathscr{I}})
,
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{K}_{\mathscr{I}})=\non(K_{\mathscr{I}})
,
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{K}_{\mathscr{I}})=\cov(K_{\mathscr{I}})
.

We will make use of the preservation theory presented in
[
CM19
]
, which is a generalization of the classical preservation theory in
[
JS90
]
and
[
Bre91
]
.

Definition 5.11

(
[
CM19
, Definition 4.1]
)
.

A relational system
\mathbf{R}=\langle X,Y,\sqsubset\rangle
is called a
Polish relational system
if the following hold:

(1)

X
is a perfect Polish space.

(2)

Y
is analytic subset of a Polish space
Z
.

(3)

\sqsubset=\bigcup_{n<\omega}\sqsubset_{n}
for some (
\subseteq
-)increasing sequence
\langle\sqsubset_{n}:n<\omega\rangle
of closed subsets of
X\times Z
such that for any
n<\omega
and any
y\in Y
,
\{x\in X\colon x\sqsubset_{n}y\}
is closed nowhere dense subset of
X
.

When dealing with a Polish relational system, we interpret it depending on the model we are working in.

Definition 5.12

(
[
JS90
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{R}=\langle X,Y,\sqsubset\rangle
be a Polish relational system. A poset
\mathbb{P}
is
\theta
-
\mathbf{R}
-good
if for any
\mathbb{P}
-name
\dot{y}
for a member of
Y
, there is a non-empty set
Y_{0}\subseteq Y
of size
<\theta
such that for any
x\in X
, if
x
is not met by any
y\in Y_{0}
, then
\mathbb{P}
forces
x
is not met by
\dot{y}
. If
\theta=\aleph_{1}
, we say “
\mathbf{R}
-good” instead of “
\aleph_{1}
-
\mathbf{R}
-good”.

Fact 5.13

(
[
Mej13
, Lemma 4]
,
[
BJ95
, Theorem 6.4.7]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{R}=\langle X,Y,\sqsubset\rangle
be a Polish relational system. Every poset of size
<\theta
is
\theta
-
\mathbf{R}
-good. In particular, Cohen forcing is
\mathbf{R}
-good.

Fact 5.14

(
[
JS90
]
,
[
CM19
, Corollary 4.13]
,
[
BCM25
, Corollary 4.10]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{R}=\langle X,Y,\sqsubset\rangle
be a Polish relational system. Then any finite support iteration of ccc
\theta
-
\mathbf{R}
-good posets is
\theta
-
\mathbf{R}
-good.

The following explains how
\mathbf{R}
-goodness is useful to control the cardinal invariants associated to
\mathbf{R}
.

Fact 5.15

(
[
CM22
, Lemma 2.15]
,
[
BCM25
, Theorem 4.11]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{R}=\langle X,Y,\sqsubset\rangle
be a Polish relational system and
\theta
be an uncountable regular cardinal. Let
\mathbb{P}
be the finite support iteration of non-trivial ccc
\theta
-
\mathbf{R}
-good posets of length
\gamma\geq\theta
. Then
\mathbb{P}
forces
C_{[\gamma]^{<\theta}}\preceq_{T}\mathbf{R}
.

We need goodness results for two specific Polish relational systems. One is associated with the constant evasion/prediction numbers discussed in
Section

4.2.1
, and the other is associated with the usual bounding/dominating numbers. Recall the notions introduced in
4.18
.

Definition 5.16
.

Let
k\geq 2
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{CPR}_{2}(k)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\langle 2^{\omega},\Pred_{2},\sqsubset^{\mathrm{pc},k}\rangle.

\displaystyle\mathbf{CPR}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\langle 2^{\omega},\Pred_{2},\sqsubset^{\mathrm{pc}}\rangle.

These are Polish relational systems. Also, note that
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{CPR}_{2}(k))=\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}(k)
,
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{CPR}_{2}(k))=\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}(k)
,
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{CPR}_{2})=\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{CPR}_{2})=\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}
.

Brendle and Shelah proved the following lemma when
\mu=\omega
, but their proof can be easily generalized.

Lemma 5.17

(
[
BS03
]
, see also
[
CR25
, Lemma 5.29]
)
.

Let
2\leq k<\omega
and
\mu
be an infinite cardinal. Then every
\mu
-
2^{k}
-linked
7
7

7

For
n\geq 2
, a subset
Q
of a poset
\mathbb{P}
is
n
-linked
if any
n
-many conditions of
Q
have a common extension.
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu
-
n
-linked
if it is a union of
\mu
-many
n
-linked components.
poset is
\mu^{+}
-
\mathbf{CPR}_{2}(k)
-good.

This lemma implies
\mathbf{CPR}_{2}
-goodness as follows:

Corollary 5.18
.

Assume that
\mu
is an infinite cardinal and a poset
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu
-
l
-linked for any
l<\omega
. Then
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu^{+}
-
\mathbf{CPR}_{2}
-good.

Proof.

Let
\dot{\pi}
be a
\mathbb{P}
-name of a member of
\Pred_{2}
. For each
k\geq 2
, let
\Pi(k)\subseteq\Pred_{2}
of size
\leq\mu
induced by
5.17
since
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu
-
2^{k}
-linked. To see
\Pi\coloneqq\bigcup_{k\geq 2}\Pi(k)
witnesses that
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu^{+}
-
\mathbf{CPR}_{2}
-good, take
x\in 2^{\omega}
such that
\lnot(x\sqsubset^{\mathrm{pc}}\pi)
for any
\pi\in\Pi
. Assume towards contradiction that
p\Vdash x\sqsubset^{\mathrm{pc}}\dot{\pi}
for some
p\in\mathbb{P}
. Then there are
q\leq p
and
k\geq 2
such that
q\Vdash x\sqsubset^{\mathrm{pc},k}\dot{\pi}
, which contradicts that
\Pi(k)
witnesses
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu^{+}
-
\mathbf{CPR}_{2}(k)
-good.
∎

We now introduce the relational system for
\mathfrak{b}
and
\mathfrak{d}
.

Definition 5.19
.

Define the relational system
\mathbf{D}\coloneqq\langle{\omega}^{\omega},{\omega}^{\omega},\leq^{*}\rangle
. Note that this is a Polish relational system and
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{D})=\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{D})=\mathfrak{d}
.

To treat
\mathbf{D}
-goodness, we will make use of the notion of
Fr-limits
, introduced by Mejía
[
Mej19
]
.

Definition 5.20
.

Let
\mathbb{P}
be a poset.

(1)

For a countable sequence
\bar{p}=\langle p_{m}:m<\omega\rangle\in\mathbb{P}^{\omega}
, we define
\dot{W}(\bar{p})
as the
\mathbb{P}
-name of an index set of the sequence
\bar{p}
as follows:

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}\dot{W}(\bar{p})\coloneq\{m<\omega:p_{m}\in\dot{G}\}

where
\dot{G}
denotes the canonical
\mathbb{P}
-name of a generic filter.

(2)

Q\subseteq\mathbb{P}
is
Fr-linked
if there exists a function
\lim\colon Q^{\omega}\to\mathbb{P}
such that for any countable sequence

\bar{q}\in Q^{\omega}
,

(5.1)

\textstyle{\lim\bar{q}}\Vdash|\dot{W}(\bar{q})|=\omega.

Additionally, if
\ran(\lim)\subseteq Q
, we say
Q
is closed-Fr-linked.

(3)

For an infinite cardinal
\mu
,
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu
-(closed-)Fr-linked
if it is a union of
\mu
-many (closed-)Fr-linked components. When
\mu=\aleph_{0}
, we use
\sigma
instead as usual. Define
<\mu
-(closed-)Fr-linkedness in the same way (for uncountable
\mu
).

We often say “
\mathbb{P}
has (closed-)Fr-limits” instead of “
\mathbb{P}
is
\sigma
-(closed-)Fr-linked”.

Lemma 5.21
.

Every poset
\mathbb{P}
is closed-
|\mathbb{P}|
-Fr-linked. In particular, Cohen forcing
\mathbb{C}
has closed-Fr-limits.

Fact 5.22

(
[
Mej19
]
)
.

Any
\mu
-Fr-linked poset is
\mu^{+}
-
\mathbf{D}
-good for any infinite cardinal
\mu
. In particular, any
<\mu
-Fr-linked poset is
\mu
-
\mathbf{D}
-good for any uncountable cardinal
\mu
.

We will now present a stronger property using (non-principal) ultrafilters, introduced in
[
GMS16
]
, which will be used to get our desired model of Cichoń’s maximum in
Section

5.3
.

Definition 5.23
.

Let
D
be an ultrafilter and
\mathbb{P}
be a poset.

(1)

A subset
Q\subseteq\mathbb{P}
is
D
-lim-linked
if there exist a
\mathbb{P}
-name
\dot{D}^{\prime}
of an ultrafilter extending
D
and a function
\lim^{D}\colon Q^{\omega}\to\mathbb{P}
such that for any countable sequence
\bar{q}\in Q^{\omega}
,

(5.2)

\textstyle{\lim^{D}\bar{q}}\Vdash\dot{W}(\bar{q})\in\dot{D}^{\prime}.

Additionally, if
\ran(\lim^{D})\subseteq Q
, we say that
Q
is
closed-
D
-lim-linked
.

(2)

A subset
Q\subseteq\mathbb{P}
is
(closed-)UF-lim-linked
if it is (closed-)
D
-lim-linked for any ultrafilter
D
.

(3)

For an infinite cardinal
\mu
,
\mathbb{P}
is
\mu
-(closed-)UF-lim-linked
if it is a union of
\mu
-many (closed-)UF-lim-linked components. When
\mu=\aleph_{0}
, we use
\sigma
instead as usual. Define
<\mu
-(closed-)UF-lim-linkedness in the same way (for uncountable
\mu
).

We often say “
\mathbb{P}
has (closed-)UF-limits” instead of “
\mathbb{P}
is
\sigma
-(closed-)UF-lim-linked”.

The following forcing-free characterization of
D
-lim-linkedness given by the fourth author will be useful.

Lemma 5.24

(
[
Yam25
, Lem 3.28]
)
.

Let
D
be an ultrafilter,
\mathbb{P}
a poset,
Q\subseteq\mathbb{P}
,
\lim^{D}\colon Q^{\omega}\to\mathbb{P}
. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\lim^{D}
witnesses
Q
is
D
-lim-linked.

(2)

\lim^{D}
satisfies
(\star)_{k}
for all
k<\omega
, where

\displaystyle(\star)_{k}:

\displaystyle\text{``Given }\bar{q}^{j}=\langle q_{m}^{j}:m<\omega\rangle\in Q^{\omega}\text{ for }j<k\text{ and }r\leq\textstyle{\lim^{D}}\bar{q}^{j}\text{ for all }j<k,

\displaystyle\text{then }\{m<\omega:r\text{ and all }q_{m}^{j}\text{ for }j<k\text{ have a common extension}\}\in D\text{''}.

This characterization is also useful to prove Fr-linkedness:

Lemma 5.25
.

Let
D
be an ultrafilter,
\mathbb{P}
a poset,
Q\subseteq\mathbb{P}
and
\lim^{D}\colon Q^{\omega}\to\mathbb{P}
.
If
\lim^{D}
satisfies
(\star)_{1}
in Lemma
5.24
, then
\lim^{D}
witnesses that
Q
is Fr-linked.

Proof.

By
(\star)_{1}
and since
D
is non-principal,

\text{if }\bar{q}=\langle q_{m}:m<\omega\rangle\in Q^{\omega}\text{ and }r\leq\textstyle{\lim^{D}}\bar{q},\text{ then we have }\exists^{\infty}m<\omega~\exists s\leq r,q_{m}.

Thus
\textstyle{\lim^{D}\bar{q}}\Vdash\exists^{\infty}m<\omega~q_{m}\in\dot{G}
.
∎

We will use closed-Fr-linkedness to control the values of
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})
and
\cov(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})
, not by goodness properties but by directly using closed-Fr-limits (
5.40
). To this end, we formulate a finite support iteration of
{<}\kappa
-closed-Fr-linked forcings. The formalization is based on
[
Mej19
]
,
[
CGHY24
]
.

Definition 5.26
.

Let
\kappa
be an uncountable regular cardinal.

•

A finite support iteration
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}=\langle(\mathbb{P}_{\xi},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\xi}):\xi<\gamma\rangle
of ccc forcings is a
{<}\kappa
-closed-Fr-iteration with witnesses
\langle\theta_{\xi}:\xi<\gamma\rangle
and
\langle\dot{Q}_{\xi,\zeta}:\zeta<\theta_{\xi},\xi<\gamma\rangle
if for any
\xi<\gamma
,
\theta_{\xi}
is a cardinal
<\kappa
and

\langle\dot{Q}_{\xi,\zeta}:\zeta<\theta_{\xi}\rangle
are
\mathbb{P}_{\xi}
-names satisfying:

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}\dot{Q}_{\xi,\zeta}\subseteq\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\xi}\text{ is closed-Fr-linked for }\zeta<\theta_{\xi}\text{ and }\bigcup_{\zeta<\theta_{\xi}}\dot{Q}_{\xi,\zeta}=\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\xi}.

•

A condition
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
is
determined
if for each
\xi\in\dom(p)
, there is
\zeta_{\xi}<\theta_{\xi}
such that
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}p(\xi)\in\dot{Q}_{\xi,\zeta_{\xi}}
. Note that there are densely many determined conditions (proved by induction on
\gamma
).

Closed-Fr-limits for conditions of the iteration
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
are defined for “refined” sequences:

Definition 5.27
.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
be a
{<}\kappa
-closed-Fr-iteration and
\delta
be an ordinal.
We say that
\bar{p}=\langle p_{m}:m<\delta\rangle\in(\mathbb{P}_{\gamma})^{\delta}
is
a uniform
\Delta
-system
if:

(1)

Each
p_{m}
is determined, witnessed by
\langle\zeta^{m}_{\xi}:\xi\in\dom(p_{m})\rangle
,

(2)

the family
\{\dom(p_{m}):m<\delta\}
is a
\Delta
-system with root
\nabla
,

(3)

there is a sequence
\langle\zeta_{\xi}^{*}:\xi\in\nabla\rangle
such that for
\xi\in\nabla
,
\zeta_{\xi}^{*}=\zeta^{m}_{\xi}
for all
m<\omega
, i.e., all
p_{m}(\xi)
are forced to be in a common closed-Fr-linked component for
\xi\in\nabla
,

(4)

all
\dom(p_{m})
have
n^{\prime}
elements, and
\dom(p_{m})=\{\alpha_{n,m}:n<n^{\prime}\}
is the increasing enumeration,

(5)

there is
r^{\prime}\subseteq n^{\prime}
such that
n\in r^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{n,m}\in\nabla
for
n<n^{\prime}
,

(6)

for
n\in n^{\prime}\setminus r^{\prime}
,
\langle\alpha_{n,m}:m<\delta\rangle
is (strictly) increasing.

Definition 5.28
.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
be a
{<}\kappa
-closed-Fr-iteration and
\bar{p}=\langle p_{m}:m<\omega\rangle\in(\mathbb{P}_{\gamma})^{\omega}
be a uniform
\Delta
-system with root
\nabla
. We (inductively) define
p^{\infty}=\lim\bar{p}
as follows:

(1)

\dom(p^{\infty})\coloneq\nabla
,

(2)

p^{\infty}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}\xi\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\xi}}p^{\infty}(\xi)\coloneq\lim\langle p_{m}(\xi):m\in\dot{W}(\bar{p}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}\xi)\rangle
for
\xi\in\nabla
, where
\bar{p}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}\xi\coloneq\langle p_{m}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}\xi:m<\omega\rangle\in(\mathbb{P}_{\xi})^{\omega}
.

To see that the second item is valid,
\dot{W}(\bar{p}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}\xi)
has to be infinite, which is true:

Lemma 5.29

(
[
Mej19
]
,
[
CGHY24
, Lemma 3.6]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
be a
{<}\kappa
-closed-Fr-iteration. Let
\bar{p}=\langle p_{m}:m<\omega\rangle\in(\mathbb{P}_{\gamma})^{\omega}
be a uniform
\Delta
-system and
p^{\infty}\coloneq\lim\bar{p}
.
Then

p^{\infty}\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}}\lvert\dot{W}(\bar{p})\rvert=\omega
.

The following is specific for closedness:

Lemma 5.30
.

Let
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
be a
{<}\kappa
-closed-Fr-iteration. Let
\bar{p}=\langle p_{m}:m<\omega\rangle\in(\mathbb{P}_{\gamma})^{\omega}
be a uniform
\Delta
-system with parameters as in
5.27
. Then
\lim\bar{p}
is determined, witnessed by
\langle\zeta^{*}_{\xi}:\xi\in\nabla\rangle
.

Proof.

Direct from the definitions of
\lim\bar{p}
and closedness.
∎

5.2.
Separations into two values

In this subsection, we study the separation of cardinal invariants into two values.

5.2.1.
The poset
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
and Fr-limits.

We have seen the connection between
\non(M_{\mathscr{I}})
(and
\cov(M_{\mathscr{I}})
) and the constant evasion (and prediction) number in
Section

4.2
. In
4.23
, we have shown that
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})=\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{\leq}(2)
, and in
4.20
, we proved that
\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\leq\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})
, so
\max\{\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2),\mathfrak{b}\}\leq\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})
. We will show that equality cannot be proved.

We introduce the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
which generically adds an
\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{ED}}
-dominating function
\phi_{G}\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\to\mathcal{ED}
.

Definition 5.31
.

The poset
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
is defined as follows: Its conditions are
p=(\sigma^{p},s^{p},n^{p},F^{p})
, where

•

\sigma^{p}\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<n^{p}}\to\omega
is a finite partial function,

•

s^{p}\colon\{(u^{\frown}a,v):(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<n^{p}},a\in\omega\}\to\omega
is a finite partial function,

•

n^{p}\in\omega

•

F^{p}\subseteq{\omega}^{\omega}
is finite.

The order
q\leq p
is given by

•

\sigma^{q}\supseteq\sigma^{p}
,
s^{q}\supseteq s^{p}
,
n^{q}\geq n^{p}
and
F^{q}\supseteq F^{p}
,

•

\forall i\in\left[n^{p},n^{q}\right)\forall x,y\in F^{p}
, either:

–

(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)\in\dom(\sigma^{q})
and
x(i)\leq\sigma^{q}(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
, or

–

(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1),y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)\in\dom(s^{q})
and
y(i)=s^{q}(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1),y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
.

Let
G\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
be a generic filter. In
V[G]
,
\phi_{G}\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\to\omega\times\omega
is given by:

\phi_{G}(u,v)\coloneqq\bigcup_{p\in G}\{(a,b)\in\omega\times\omega:a\leq\sigma^{p}(u,v)\text{ or }b=s^{p}(u^{\frown}a,v)\}.

One can easily show the following.

Lemma 5.32
.

•

For
n<\omega
,
\{p:n_{p}\geq n\}
is dense. Thus
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}\Vdash\dom(\phi_{G})=(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}
.

•

For
(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}
,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}\Vdash\forall^{\infty}n<\omega~|(\phi_{G}(u,v))_{n}|=1
, so
\ran(\phi_{G})\subseteq\mathcal{ED}
.

•

For
f,g\in{\omega}^{\omega}
,
\{p:f,g\in F^{p}\}
is dense.
Thus
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}\Vdash\forall(f,g)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{\omega}\cap V,~(f,g)\in M_{\phi_{G}}
, i.e.,
(\omega\times\omega)^{\omega}\cap V\subseteq M_{\phi_{G}}
.

\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
is
\sigma
-centered and
\sigma
-Fr-linked.

Lemma 5.33
.

Fix
n\in\omega
,
\sigma\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<n}\to\omega
finite partial function and
s\colon\{(u^{\frown}a,v):(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<n},a\in\omega\}\to\omega
finite partial function. Let
L<\omega
and
\bar{x}^{*}=\{x^{*}_{l}:l<L\}\subseteq\omega^{n}
such that all
x^{*}_{l}
are pairwise different.
Then

Q\coloneqq\{p\in\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}\colon\sigma^{p}=\sigma,s^{p}=s,n^{p}=n,\{x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n\colon x\in F^{p}\}=\bar{x}^{*}\}

is centered and Fr-linked. In particular,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
is
\sigma
-centered and
\sigma
-Fr-linked.

Proof.

Centeredness is clear. To prove
Q
is Fr-linked, take some ultrafilter
D
, we will show
(\star)_{1}
as in
5.25
.
For
\bar{x}=\langle x^{m}\in{\omega}^{\omega}:m<\omega\rangle
,
define a partial function
\bar{x}^{\infty}\colon\omega\to\omega
as follows: for each
i<\omega
, if there (uniquely) exists
a_{i}<\omega
such that
\{m<\omega:x^{i}(m)=a_{i}\}\in D
,
let
i\in\dom(\bar{x}^{\infty})
and
\bar{x}^{\infty}(i)\coloneq a_{i}
. Otherwise let
i\notin\dom(\bar{x}^{\infty})
.

For
\bar{q}=\langle q_{m}=(\sigma,s,n,F_{m}=\{x^{m}_{l}:l<L\}):m<\omega\rangle\in Q^{\omega}
,
we define
\lim^{D}\bar{q}\coloneqq q^{\infty}\coloneqq(\sigma^{\infty},s^{\infty},n^{\infty},F^{\infty})
as follows:

•

\bar{x}_{l}\coloneq\langle x^{m}_{l}:m<\omega\rangle
,
A\coloneq\{l<k:{\dom(\bar{x}_{l}^{\infty}})=\omega\},B\coloneq L\setminus A
.

•

F^{\infty}\coloneq\{{\bar{x}_{l}^{\infty}}:l\in A\}
.

•

For
l\in B
,
n_{l}\coloneqq\min(\omega\setminus\dom(\bar{x}_{l}^{\infty}))

(hence
n_{l}\geq n
). For
l\in A
, put
n_{l}\coloneqq\omega
for convenience.

•

n^{\infty}\coloneqq n\cup\max\{n_{l}+1:l\in B\}
.

•

For each
l\in B
, let
t_{l}\in\omega^{n_{l}}
be such that

X_{0}^{l}\coloneqq\{m<\omega:x_{l}^{m}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{l}=t_{l}\}\in D.

Put
X_{0}\coloneqq\bigcap_{l\in B}X_{0}^{l}\in D
.

•

For
i<\omega
,
put

W_{i}\coloneqq\{\bar{x}_{l}^{\infty}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i:l\in A\}\cup\{t_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i:l\in B,i\leq n_{l}\}.

Note that
W_{i}
is a finite subset of
\omega^{i}
.

•

Take a finite partial function
\sigma^{\infty}\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<n^{p}}\to\omega
such that:

(1)

\sigma^{\infty}\supseteq\sigma
.

(2)

For any
i\in\left[n,n^{\infty}\right)
and any
u\in W_{i+1}
and
v\in W_{i}
,
(u\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,v)\in\dom(\sigma^{\infty})
and
u(i)\leq\sigma^{\infty}(u\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,v)
.

•

s^{\infty}\coloneqq s
.

Clearly
q^{\infty}
is a valid condition,
so it is enough to show that
\lim^{D}
satisfies
(\star)_{1}
.
Assume that
r\leq q^{\infty}=(\sigma^{\infty},s^{\infty},n^{\infty},F^{\infty})
.
Let
r\coloneq(\sigma^{r},s^{r},n^{r},F^{r})
.
For
l\in A
, put

X_{1}^{l}\coloneqq\{m<\omega:x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n^{r}+1)=x^{\infty}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n^{r}+1)\}\in D.

Let
X_{1}\coloneqq\bigcap\{X_{1}^{l}:l\in A\}\in D
.
For
l\in B
and
l^{\prime}<L
, let

X_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{m<\omega:(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{l}+1),x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{l})\notin\dom(s^{r})\}\in D.

Put
X_{2}\coloneqq\bigcap\{X_{2}^{l}:l\in B,l^{\prime}<L\}\in D
.
For
l\in B
and
i>n_{l}
, let

X_{3}^{l,i}\coloneqq\{m<\omega:x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i\notin\operatorname{proj}_{1}(\dom(\sigma^{r}))\cup\operatorname{proj}_{2}(\dom(\sigma^{r}))\}\in D,

where
\operatorname{proj}_{1}(\dom(\sigma^{r}))\coloneqq\dom(\dom(\sigma^{r}))
and
\operatorname{proj}_{2}(\dom(\sigma^{r}))\coloneqq\ran(\dom(\sigma^{r}))
.
Put
X_{3}\coloneqq\bigcap\{X_{3}^{l,i}:l\in B,i\in\left(n_{l},n^{r}\right)\}\in D
. Put
X\coloneq X_{0}\cap X_{1}\cap X_{2}\cap X_{3}\in D
.

It is enough to show that for all
m\in X
,
q_{m}
and
r
are compatible.
Fix such
m
. Note that
\sigma^{r}\supseteq\sigma^{\infty}\supseteq\sigma
and
s^{r}\supseteq s^{\infty}\supseteq s
.
Take a condition
q^{\prime}\coloneq(\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime},n^{\prime},F^{\prime})
(as a common extension of
q_{m}
and
r
) such that:

•

F^{\prime}\coloneq F^{r}\cup F_{m}
.

•

n^{\prime}\coloneqq n^{r}
.

•

\sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\sigma^{r}
and for all
(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\setminus\dom(\sigma^{r})
of the form
(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
for some
l,l^{\prime}<L
and
i<n^{r}
, let
(u,v)\in\dom(\sigma^{\prime})
and:

(5.3)

\sigma^{\prime}(u,v)\coloneqq\max\{x^{m}_{l^{*}}(i):l^{*}<L\}.

Note that there are only finitely many such
(u,v)
.

•

s^{\prime}\supseteq s^{r}
and for all
(u^{\prime},v)\in\omega^{<\omega}\times\omega^{<\omega}\setminus\dom(s^{r})
of the form
(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{l}+1),x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{l})
for some
l\in B
and
l^{\prime}<L
,
let
(u^{\prime},v)\in\dom(s^{\prime})
and:

(5.4)

s^{\prime}(u^{\prime},v)\coloneqq x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}(n_{l}),

which is possible since all
x^{*}_{l}
are pairwise distinct. Note that there are only finitely many such
(u^{\prime},v)
.

By the choice of
X_{3}
, for any
l\in B
,
i\in\left(n_{l},n^{r}\right)
and
l^{\prime}<L
, we have

(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i),(x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)\notin\dom(\sigma^{r})
and hence

(5.5)

x^{m}_{l}(i)\leq\sigma^{\prime}(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)\text{ and }x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}(i)\leq\sigma^{\prime}(x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i).

By the choice of
X_{2}
, for any
l\in B
and
l^{\prime}<L
we have
(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{l}+1),x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{l})\notin\dom(s^{r})
and hence

(5.6)

x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}(n_{l})=s^{\prime}(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{l}+1),x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{l}).

q^{\prime}\leq r
trivially holds.
To see
q^{\prime}\leq q_{m}
, let
l,l^{\prime}<L
and
i\in\left[n,n^{\prime}\right)
.
We show that:

(5.7)

\text{either }x^{m}_{l}(i)\leq\sigma^{\prime}(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)\text{ or }x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}(i)=s^{\prime}(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1),x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i).

First assume
l,l^{\prime}\in A
. If
i\in\left[n,n^{\infty}\right)
, by the choice of
X_{1}
,
x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1)=\bar{x}^{\infty}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1)\in W_{i+1}
and
x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i=\bar{x}^{\infty}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i\in W_{i}
. Thus
x^{m}_{l}(i)\leq\sigma^{\infty}(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)=\sigma^{\prime}(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
. If
i\in\left[n^{\infty},n^{r}\right)
, (
5.7
) follows from
r\leq q^{\infty}
and by the choice of
X_{1}
.

Thus we may assume either
l\in B
or
l^{\prime}\in B
. If
i\in\left(n_{l},n^{r}\right)
or
i\in\left(n_{l^{\prime}},n^{r}\right)
, we are done by (
5.5
).
If
i\notin\left(n_{l},n^{r}\right)
and
i\notin\left(n_{l^{\prime}},n^{r}\right)
, we particularly have
i\in\left[n,n^{\infty}\right)
.
Put
u\coloneqq x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1)
and
v\coloneqq x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i
. There are three cases.

(i)

If
u\in W_{i+1}
and
v\in W_{i}
, then
(u\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,v)\in\dom(\sigma^{\infty})
and
u(i)\leq\sigma^{\infty}(u\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,v)=\sigma^{\prime}(u\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,v)
by the choice of
\sigma^{\infty}
, so (
5.7
) is true.

(ii)

If
v\notin W_{i}
, then
l^{\prime}\in B
holds, since otherwise
v=x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i=\bar{x}^{\infty}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i\in W_{i}
by the choice of
X_{1}
. Since
i\notin\left(n_{l^{\prime}},n^{r}\right)
, we have
i\leq n_{l^{\prime}}
. By the choice of
X_{0}
,
v=x_{l^{\prime}}^{m}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i=t_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i\in W_{i}
, a contradiction.

(iii)

If
u\notin W_{i+1}
, for the same reason as the previous case,
l\in B
and
i+1>n_{l}
since otherwise we would obtain
u\in W_{i+1}
. Since
i\notin\left(n_{l},n^{r}\right)
, we have
i=n_{l}
. In this case
x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}(n_{l})=s^{\prime}(x^{m}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{l}+1),x^{m}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{l})
by (
5.6
) and hence (
5.7
) is true.

Therefore, (
5.7
) is satisfied in any case and hence
q^{\prime}\leq q_{m}
.
∎

Brendle introduced a forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\omega}
which adds a generic predictor on the
2
-constant prediction:

Fact 5.34

(
[
Bre03
]
)
.

For
2\leq k<\omega
, there is a
\sigma
-linked poset
\mathbb{P}^{\omega}
such that:

\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}^{\omega}}\exists\dot{\pi}\in\Pred~\forall x\in{\omega}^{\omega}\cap V~(x\sqsubset^{\mathrm{pc},k}\dot{\pi}).

Now we prove the consistency of
\max\{\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\}<\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})
(and the dual).

Theorem 5.35
.

(1)

Given
\kappa<\lambda=\lambda^{<\kappa}
regular uncountable, there is a ccc poset forcing
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)=\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}=\kappa
and
\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})=\mathfrak{c}=\lambda
.
In particular,
\max\{\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\}<\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})
is consistent.

(2)

Given
\kappa
regular uncountable and
\lambda=\lambda^{\omega}>\kappa
, there is a ccc poset forcing
\cov(M_{\mathcal{ED}})=\kappa
and
\mathfrak{d}=\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}=\lambda=\mathfrak{c}
. In particular,
\cov(M_{\mathcal{ED}})<\min\{\mathfrak{d},\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\}
is consistent.

Proof.

(1)

Using a bookkeeping argument, craft
\mathbb{P}
a finite support iteration of length
\lambda
whose iterands are either:

(a)

\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
,

(b)

a subforcing of Hechler forcing of size
<\kappa
, or

(c)

a subforcing of
\mathbb{P}^{\omega}
of size
<\kappa
.

such that
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
appears cofinally, and all possible witnesses for
\mathfrak{b}
and
\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)
of size
<\kappa
are destroyed.
In the
V^{\mathbb{P}}
-extension, clearly
\lambda\leq\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})\leq\mathfrak{c}
and
\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\geq\kappa
. By
5.21
and
5.33
, every iterand is
<\kappa
-Fr-linked and hence
\kappa
-
\mathbf{D}
-good by
5.22
.
Thus
\mathbb{P}
is also
\kappa
-
\mathbf{D}
-good by
5.14
, so we have
\mathfrak{b}\leq\kappa
by
5.15
. Similarly, by
5.33
, every iterand is
<\kappa
-
l
-linked for any
l<\omega
,
\mathbb{P}
is
\kappa
-
\mathbf{CPR}_{2}
-good by
5.18
and hence
\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\leq\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}\leq\kappa
.

(2)

Let
\mathbb{P}
be a finite support iteration of length
\lambda+\kappa
such that the first
\lambda
-many iterands are Cohen forcings and each of the rest is
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
. It is easy to see that
\mathbb{P}
forces
\kappa\leq\cov(\mathcal{M})\leq\cov(M_{\mathcal{ED}})\leq\kappa
and
\mathfrak{c}\leq\lambda
.
Since every iterand is
\mathbf{D}
-good and
\mathbf{CPR}_{2}
-good, we have
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}}\mathfrak{d},\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}\geq\lambda
by
5.15
.

∎

5.2.2.
The poset
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
and closed-Fr-limits.

Let
\langle I_{i}=\left[M_{i},M_{i+1}\right)\rangle_{i<\omega}
be the interval partition of
\omega
with
|I_{i}|=i
and we consider
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
on this interval partition. We introduce the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
which generically adds a
\mathbf{K}_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
-dominating function
\phi_{G}\colon\omega\to\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
.

Definition 5.36
.

A forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
is defined as follows:

•

Conditions are
p=(i,k,s,\varphi)=(i_{p},k_{p},s_{p},\varphi_{p})
where
i,k<\omega
,
s\in([M_{i}]^{\leq k})^{<\omega}
and
\varphi\colon\omega\to[\omega]^{\leq k}
such that
\varphi\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}|s|=s
.

•

The order
(i^{\prime},k^{\prime},s^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})\leq(i,k,s,\varphi)
is defined by:
i^{\prime}\geq i
,
k^{\prime}\geq k
,
\varphi^{\prime}(n)\supseteq\varphi(n)
for all
n<\omega
,
|s^{\prime}|\geq|s|
and for
n<|s|
,

(5.8)

\displaystyle s^{\prime}(n)\cap M_{i}=s(n),\text{ and}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\text{if }i\leq j<i^{\prime},\text{ then }|s^{\prime}(n)\cap I_{j}|\leq n.

•

For a generic filter
G
, define
\phi_{G}\colon\omega\to\mathcal{P}(\omega)
by:

\phi_{G}(n)\coloneq\bigcup\{s_{p}(n):p\in G\text{ and }n<|s|\}.

One easily sees:

Lemma 5.37
.

The following sets are open dense:

(1)

For
x\in{\omega}^{\omega}
,
\{p:x(n)\in\varphi_{p}(n)\text{ for all }n\geq|s_{p}|\}
,

(2)

for
i<\omega
,
\{p:i_{p}\geq i\}
,

(3)

for
n<\omega
,
\{p:|s_{p}|\geq n\}
.

By Lemma
5.37
(
3
), we may always assume
|s_{p}|\geq k_{p}
.
By (
5.9
) and by Lemma
5.37
(
1
), we have the following:

Lemma 5.38
.

Let
G
be a generic filter. The following are true in
V[G]
:

(1)

\forall n<\omega~\forall^{\infty}i<\omega~|\phi_{G}(n)\cap I_{i}|\leq n
. In particular,
\phi_{G}(n)\in\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
.

(2)

\forall x\in{\omega}^{\omega}\cap V~\forall^{\infty}n<\omega~x(n)\in\phi_{G}(n)
. In other words,
{\omega}^{\omega}\cap V\subseteq K_{\phi_{G}}
.

\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
is
\sigma
-centered and
\sigma
-Fr-linked:

Lemma 5.39
.

For
i,k<\omega
and
s\in([M_{i}]^{\leq k})^{<\omega}
with
|s|\geq k
,

Q=\{p\in\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}:i_{p}=i,k_{p}=k,s_{p}=s\}

is centered and closed-Fr-linked. In particular,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
is
\sigma
-centered and
\sigma
-closed-Fr-linked.

Proof.

Centeredness is clear.
To show
(\star)_{1}
in Lemma
5.25
, take some non-principal ultrafilter
D
on
\omega
and let and
\bar{q}=\langle(i,k,s,\varphi_{m})\rangle_{m<\omega}\in Q^{\omega}
.
Define
\varphi_{\infty}\colon\omega\to[\omega]^{\leq k}
by:

(5.10)

a\in\varphi_{\infty}(n):\Leftrightarrow\{m<\omega:a\in\varphi_{m}(n)\}\in D,

for
n,a<\omega
. Put
\lim^{D}\bar{q}\coloneqq q_{\infty}\coloneqq(i,k,s,\varphi_{\infty})
(so
\ran(\lim^{D})\subseteq Q
). To see
(\star)_{1}
,
assume
q^{\prime}=(i^{\prime},k^{\prime},s^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})\leq q_{\infty}
.
Put
M\coloneq M_{i}
and
M^{\prime}\coloneq M_{i^{\prime}}
.
By (
5.10
), there exists
X\in D
such that:

(5.11)

\text{For all }m\in X\text{ and }n\in|s^{\prime}|\setminus|s|,~\varphi_{m}(n)\cap M^{\prime}=\varphi_{\infty}(n),

since
\varphi_{\infty}(n)\subseteq M^{\prime}
for such
n
by
q^{\prime}\leq q_{\infty}
.
Fix
m\in X
and we will define a common extension
r
of
q^{\prime}
and
q_{m}
.
Define
\psi\colon\omega\to[\omega]^{\leq k^{\prime}+k}
by
\psi(n)\coloneqq\varphi^{\prime}(n)\cup\varphi_{m}(n)
and
t\coloneq\psi\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}|s^{\prime}|
. Note that:

(5.12)

\text{for }n<|s|,~t(n)=\varphi^{\prime}(n)\cup\varphi_{m}(n)=s^{\prime}(n)\cup s(n)=s^{\prime}(n).

Put
r\coloneq(i^{\prime\prime},k^{\prime}+k,t,\psi)
, where
i^{\prime\prime}\geq i^{\prime}
is so large that
r
is a valid condition.
To see
r\leq q^{\prime}
, it is enough to check that for
n<|t|
:

(1)

t(n)\cap M^{\prime}=s^{\prime}(n)
, and

(2)

\text{if }i^{\prime}\leq j<i^{\prime\prime},\text{ then }|t(n)\cap I_{j}|\leq n
.

We show (
1
).
If
n<|s|
, by (
5.12
) we have

t(n)\cap M^{\prime}=s^{\prime}(n)\cap M^{\prime}=s^{\prime}(n).

If
n\in|t|\setminus|s|
, by (
5.11
) we have

t(n)\cap M^{\prime}=(\varphi^{\prime}(n)\cap M^{\prime})\cup(\varphi_{m}(n)\cap M^{\prime})=\varphi^{\prime}(n)\cup\varphi_{\infty}(n)=\varphi^{\prime}(n)=s^{\prime}(n)

by
\varphi^{\prime}(n)\supseteq\varphi_{\infty}(n)
, which follows from
q^{\prime}\leq q_{\infty}
.

We show (
2
).
If
n<|s|
, then

t(n)\cap I_{j}=s^{\prime}(n)\cap I_{j}=\emptyset

by (
5.12
).
If
n\in|t|\setminus|s|
, then

t(n)\cap I_{j}=(\varphi^{\prime}(n)\cup\varphi_{m}(n))\cap I_{j}=\varphi_{m}(n)\cap I_{j}\subseteq\varphi_{m}(n)

has size
\leq k\leq|s|\leq n
.

To see
r\leq q_{m}
, it is enough to check that for
n<|s|
:

(3)

t(n)\cap M=s(n)
, and:

(4)

\text{if }i\leq j<i^{\prime\prime},\text{ then }|t(n)\cap I_{j}|\leq n
.

(
3
) follows from
t(n)\cap M=s^{\prime}(n)\cap M=s(n)
by (
5.12
) and
q^{\prime}\leq q_{\infty}
.

To show (
4
), let
i\leq j<i^{\prime\prime}
and we may assume
i\leq j<i^{\prime}
by (
2
).

t(n)\cap I_{j}=s^{\prime}(n)\cap I_{j}
by (
5.12
) and
|s^{\prime}(n)\cap I_{j}|\leq n
follows from
q^{\prime}\leq q_{\infty}
.

Therefore,
r
extends
q^{\prime}
and
q_{m}
.
∎

Closed-Fr-limits can control the values of
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})
and
\cov(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})
using Cohen reals:

Lemma 5.40
.

Let
\kappa
be an uncountable regular cardinal and
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
be a
{<}\kappa
-closed-Fr-iteration such that the first
\kappa
-many iterands are Cohen forcings. Let
\{\dot{c}_{\beta}:\beta<\kappa\}
be the added Cohen reals as members of
(\omega\times\omega)^{\omega}
. Then, for any
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
-name
\dot{\phi}
of a function
(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\to\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
,
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}}|\{\beta<\kappa:\dot{c}_{\beta}\in M_{\dot{\phi}}\}|<\kappa
.

Proof.

For
x\in(\omega\times\omega)^{\omega}
,
\phi\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\to\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
and
n<\omega
, let
x\in^{*}_{n}\phi
denote
\forall m\geq n~x(m)\in\phi(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}m)
.
Assume towards contradiction that there exists a condition
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
such that

p\Vdash|\{\beta<\kappa:\dot{c}_{\beta}\in M_{\dot{\phi}}\}|=\kappa
.
For each
i<\kappa
, inductively pick
p_{i}\leq p
,
\beta_{i}<\lambda
and
n_{i}<\omega
such that
\beta_{i}\notin\{\beta_{i^{\prime}}:i^{\prime}<i\}
and
p_{i}\Vdash\dot{c}_{\beta_{i}}\in^{*}_{n_{i}}\dot{\phi}
.
By extending and thinning, we may assume:

(1)

\beta_{i}\in\dom(p_{i})
. (By extending
p_{i}
.)

(2)

\{p_{i}:i<\kappa\}
forms a uniform
\Delta
-system with root
\nabla
.

(3)

All
n_{i}
are equal to
n^{*}
.

(4)

All
p_{i}(\beta_{i})
are the same Cohen condition
s\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}
.

(5)

|s|=n^{*}
. (By extending
s
or increasing
n^{*}
.)

In particular, we have that:

(5.13)

\text{For each }i<\kappa,p_{i}\text{ forces }\dot{c}_{\beta_{i}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n^{*}=s\text{ and }\dot{c}_{\beta_{i}}\in^{*}_{n^{*}}\dot{\phi}.

Note that
\beta_{i}\notin\nabla
for
i<\kappa
since all
\beta_{i}
are distinct. Pick the first
\omega
many
p_{i}
and fix some bijection
i\colon\omega\times\omega\to\omega
.
For each
(a,b)\in\omega\times\omega
, define
q_{a,b}\leq p_{i(a,b)}
by extending the
\beta_{i(a,b)}
-th position
q_{\sigma}(\beta_{i(a,b)}):=s^{\frown}(a,b)
.
By (
5.13
),

\text{for each }(a,b)\in\omega\times\omega,~q_{a,b}\text{ forces }\dot{c}_{\beta_{i(a,b)}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n^{*}+1)=s^{\frown}(a,b)\text{ and }\dot{c}_{\beta_{i(a,b)}}\in^{*}_{n^{*}}\dot{\phi}
,
thus

(5.14)

q_{a,b}\Vdash\dot{c}_{\beta_{i(a,b)}}(n^{*})=(a,b)\in\dot{\phi}(s)=\dot{\phi}(\dot{c}_{\beta_{i(a,b)}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n^{*}).

Fix
a<\omega
and we consider the sequence
\bar{q}_{a}\coloneq\langle q_{a,b}:b<\omega\rangle
.
When defining
q_{a,b}
we changed the
\beta_{i(a,b)}
-th position which is out of
\nabla
, so
\{q_{a,b}:b<\omega\}
forms a uniform
\Delta
-system with root
\nabla
.
Thus we can take their limit
q^{\infty}_{a}\coloneqq\lim\bar{q}_{a}
.
By (
5.14
) and
5.29
, we obtain:

(5.15)

q^{\infty}_{a}\Vdash\exists^{\infty}b<\omega~(a,b)\in\dot{\phi}(s).

Unfix
a
and consider the sequence
\bar{q}\coloneq\langle q^{\infty}_{a}:a<\omega\rangle
.
By
5.30
, all
q^{\infty}_{a}
have domain
\nabla
and they form a uniform
\Delta
-system with root
\nabla
. Take their limit
q^{\infty}\coloneqq\lim\bar{q}
.
By (
5.15
),

(5.16)

q^{\infty}\Vdash\exists^{\infty}a<\omega\exists^{\infty}b<\omega~(a,b)\in\dot{\phi}(s),

which contradicts
\dot{\phi}(s)\in\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
.
∎

Now we prove the consistency of
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})<\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
(and its dual).

Theorem 5.41
.

(1)

Given
\kappa<\lambda=\lambda^{<\kappa}
regular uncountable, there is a ccc poset forcing
\mathfrak{b}=\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})=\kappa
and
\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\mathfrak{c}=\lambda
.
In particular,
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})<\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
is consistent.

(2)

Given
\kappa
regular uncountable and
\lambda=\lambda^{\omega}>\kappa
, there is a ccc poset forcing
\cov(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\kappa
and
\cov(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})=\lambda=\mathfrak{c}
.

Proof.

(1)

Using a bookkeeping argument, craft
\mathbb{P}
a
\kappa
-closed-Fr-iteration, in such a way that the first
\kappa
iterands are Cohen forcing, and the rest are a combination of
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
or a subforcing of the Hechler forcing of size
<\kappa
so it kills all witnesses of
\mathfrak{b}
of size
<\kappa
, which is possible by
5.21
and
5.39
. It is easy to see that
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
forces
\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\mathfrak{c}=\lambda
and
\mathfrak{b}\geq\kappa
.
5.40
implies that the first
\kappa
-many Cohen reals witnesses
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})\leq\kappa
.

(2)

Let
\gamma=\lambda+\kappa
and
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
be a
<\aleph_{1}
-closed-Fr-iteration such that the first
\lambda
-many iterands are Cohen forcings and each of the rest is
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
, which is possible by
5.21
and
5.39
. It is easy to see that
\mathbb{P}
forces
\kappa\leq\cov(\mathcal{M})\leq\cov(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\kappa
and
\mathfrak{c}\leq\lambda
.
To see
\cov(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})\geq\lambda
,
let
\mu<\lambda
be an infinite cardinal and
\{\dot{\phi}_{\alpha}:\alpha<\mu\}
be
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
-names of functions
(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\to\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
.
Let
\{\dot{c}_{\beta}:\beta<\lambda\}
be Cohen reals as members of
(\omega\times\omega)^{\omega}
added at the first
\lambda
stages. By
5.40
,
\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}}|\bigcup_{\alpha<\mu}\{\beta<\lambda:\dot{c}_{\beta}\in M_{\dot{\phi}_{\alpha}}\}|\leq\mu<\lambda
, which implies that
\{M_{\dot{\phi}_{\alpha}}:\alpha<\mu\}
cannot cover
(\omega\times\omega)^{\omega}
. Thus
\cov(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})\geq\lambda
.

∎

Recall that
\max\{\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{b}(2)\}\leq\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
for any increasing function
b\in{\omega}^{\omega}
by
4.21
.
By an argument similar to the previous theorem, it follows that equality cannot be proved. Moreover, we obtain the following stronger theorem:

Theorem 5.42
.

(1)

Given
\kappa<\lambda=\lambda^{<\kappa}
regular uncountable, there is a ccc poset forcing that
\mathfrak{b}=\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)=\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}=\kappa
and
\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\mathfrak{c}=\lambda
.
In particular, for any increasing function
b\in{\omega}^{\omega}
,
\max\{\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{b}(2)\}<\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
is consistent.

(2)

Given
\kappa
regular uncountable and
\lambda=\lambda^{\omega}>\kappa
, there is a ccc poset forcing that
\cov(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\kappa
and
\mathfrak{d}=\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}=\lambda=\mathfrak{c}
.
In particular, for any increasing function
b\in{\omega}^{\omega}
,
\cov(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\cov(M_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})<\max\{\mathfrak{d},\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{b}(2)\}
is consistent.

Proof.

(1)

Note that
\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\leq\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{b}(2)\leq\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}(2)\leq\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}
for any increasing function
b\in{\omega}^{\omega}
. Perform the same iteration
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
as in
5.41
(1), but interleave subforcings of
\mathbb{P}^{\omega}
(see
5.34
) of size
<\kappa
.
Similarly,
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
forces
\mathfrak{b}=\kappa
,
\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})=\mathfrak{c}=\lambda
, and
\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\geq\kappa
. For the same reason as
5.35
,
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
forces
\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}\leq\kappa
.

(2)

Use the same
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
in
5.41
(2). For the same reason as in
5.35
,
\mathbb{P}_{\gamma}
forces
\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{2}=\lambda
and we are done.

∎

We do not know whether
\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})<\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
and
\cov(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})>\cov(M_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
are consistent or not.

5.3.
Extending Cichoń’s maximum

In this section, we construct a model of Cichoń’s maximum as described in
D
.
Following the construction of the original model of Cichoń’s maximum in
[
GKS19
]
,
[
GKMS22
]
, we shall separate the left side of the diagram (
Figure

4
) and then separate the right (
Figure

5
). The separation of the right side is obtained by just applying the method in
[
GKMS22
]
(the method in
[
GKS19
]
requires large cardinals),
so our essential goal is to separate the left side to have
Figure

4
.

Let us review the separation of the left side of (the not extended) Cichoń’s diagram by Goldstern–Mejía–Shelah
[
GMS16
]
. The main work is
to keep the bounding number
\mathfrak{b}
small
through the forcing iteration, since the naive bookkeeping iteration used to increase the cardinal invariants in the left side guarantees the smallness of the other numbers but not of
\mathfrak{b}
.
As we have seen in
Section

5.2
, Fr-limits keep
\mathfrak{b}
small, but these limits do not work here. In the bookkeeping iteration,
subforcings
of particular forcing notions are iterated, but subforcings of a poset which has Fr-limits might not have Fr-limits, and hence the smallness of
\mathfrak{b}
is not guaranteed.
However, we can overcome this problem by using the stronger limits,
UF-limits
.
By using this UF-limit method, we can choose suitable subforcings while keeping
\mathfrak{b}
small (see
[
GMS16
]
for details). This is why UF-limits are needed instead of Fr-limits.

Let us go back to the separation of the left side of our extended Cichoń’s diagram (
Figure

4
). The main work is to keep the bounding number
\mathfrak{b}
and
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})
small through the forcing iteration (it is known that there is a forcing notion denoted by
\mathbb{LE}
that keeps
\non(\mathcal{E})
small through the iteration, by
[
Yam24
]
). Although we know that Fr-limits and closed-Fr-limits keep
\mathfrak{b}
and
\non(M_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}})
small (
5.40
) respectively, we need UF-limits and closed-UF-limits as explained above.
In
Section

5.2
, we introduced the posets
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
, which have Fr-limits and closed-Fr-limits, and increase
\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})
and
\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})
, respectively (
Table

3
). However, it is unclear whether these posets have (closed-)UF-limits. More specifically, when proving that these posets have (closed-)Fr-limits, we showed
(\star)_{1}
in
5.24
, but we need to show
(\star)_{k}
for
k\geq 2
to prove that they have (closed-)UF-limits, which is unclear (see
5.47
and
5.56
).

Instead, we introduce new posets which have (closed-)UF-limits, by slightly modifying the definitions of
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
.
Consequently, we change the ideals we deal with, and we encounter two ideals:
\mathscr{J}_{L}
and
\mathscr{I}^{f}_{L}
(
Table

3
).
These ideals themselves have interesting properties which are not directly related to (closed-)UF-limits (we study their properties in
Section

6
), so our use of these ideals does not seem ad hoc.
In this section, we introduce the ideals
\mathscr{J}_{L}
and
\mathscr{I}^{f}_{L}
, and the posets
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
, which increase
\non(M_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})
and
\non(K_{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}})
and have UF-limits and closed-UF-limits, respectively. In the end, we construct our desired model of Cichoń’s maximum in
5.61
using these posets.

Table 2.
Posets with (closed-)Fr-limits

posets

increase

limits

\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}

\non(M_{\mathcal{ED}})

Fr-limits

\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}

\non(K_{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}})

closed-Fr-limits

Table 3.
Posets with (closed-)UF-limits

posets

increase

limits

\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}

\non(M_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})

UF-limits

\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}

\non(K_{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}})

closed-UF-limits

5.3.1.
The ideal
\mathscr{J}_{L}
and the poset
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}

We change the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
so that it will have UF-limits. The following ideal was already in the introduction, and we calculated some of its associated cardinal invariants in
Section

3
.

Definition 5.43
.

The ideal
\mathscr{J}_{L}
on
\omega\times\omega
is defined by

\mathscr{J}_{L}=\{A\subseteq\omega\times\omega:\exists k<\omega~\forall^{\infty}i<\omega~\lvert(A)_{i}\rvert\leq k\cdot i\}.

By definition,
\mathcal{ED}\subseteq\mathscr{J}_{L}\subseteq\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
. The ideal
\mathscr{J}_{L}
is to the linear growth ideal
\mathscr{I}_{L}
what the eventually different ideal
\mathcal{ED}
is to
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
. Namely,
\mathscr{J}_{L}\upharpoonright\Delta_{\mathrm{exp}}
, where
\Delta_{\mathrm{exp}}=\{\langle i,j\rangle:j\leq\exp(i)\}
, is isomorphic to
\mathscr{I}_{L}
. It follows that
\mathscr{J}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}_{L}
. Ideals between
\mathcal{ED}
and
\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
in the sense of Katětov order (including
\mathscr{J}_{L}
) are extensively studied in
[
DFGT21
]
.

We introduce the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
which generically adds an
\mathbf{M}_{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
-dominating function
\phi_{G}\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\to\mathscr{J}_{L}
.
The poset is obtained by slightly changing the definition of
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
.

Definition 5.44
.

The poset
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
is defined as follows: Its conditions are
p=(\sigma^{p},s^{p},n^{p},F^{p})
such that

•

\sigma^{p}\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<n^{p}}\to\omega
is a finite partial function,

•

s^{p}\colon\{(u^{\frown}a,v):(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<n^{p}},a\in\omega\}\to[\omega]^{<\omega}
is a finite partial function such that
|s^{p}(u^{\frown}a,v)|\leq a
for
(u^{\frown}a,v)\in\dom(s^{p})
,

•

n^{p}\in\omega
,

•

F^{p}\subseteq{\omega}^{\omega}
is finite.

The order
q\leq p
is given by

•

\sigma^{q}\supseteq\sigma^{p}
,
s^{q}\supseteq s^{p}
,
n^{q}\geq n^{p}
and
F^{q}\supseteq F^{p}
,

•

\forall i\in\left[n^{p},n^{q}\right)\forall x,y\in F^{p}
, either:

–

(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)\in\dom(\sigma^{q})
and
x(i)\leq\sigma^{q}(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
, or

–

(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1),y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)\in\dom(s^{q})
and
y(i)\in s^{q}(x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1),y\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
.

Let
G
be a generic filter. In
V[G]
,
\phi_{G}\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\to\omega\times\omega
is given by

\phi_{G}(u,v)\coloneqq\bigcup_{p\in G}\{(a,b)\in\omega\times\omega:a\leq\sigma^{p}(u,v)\text{ or }b\in s^{p}(u^{\frown}a,v)\}.

As in the case of
5.32
,

Lemma 5.45
.

(1)

For
n<\omega
,
\{p:n_{p}\geq n\}
is dense. Thus
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}\Vdash\dom(\phi_{G})=(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}
.

(2)

For
(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}
,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}\Vdash\forall^{\infty}n<\omega~|(\phi_{G}(u,v))_{n}|\leq n
, so
\ran(\phi_{G})\subseteq\mathscr{J}_{L}
.

(3)

For
f,g\in{\omega}^{\omega}
,
\{p:f,g\in F^{p}\}
is dense.
Thus
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}\Vdash(\omega\times\omega)^{\omega}\cap V\subseteq M_{\phi_{G}}
.

\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
is
\sigma
-centered and has UF-limits:

Lemma 5.46
.

Fix
n\in\omega
,
\sigma\colon(\omega\times\omega)^{<n}\to\omega
a finite partial function, and
s\colon\{(u^{\frown}a,v):(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<n},a\in\omega\}\to\omega
a finite partial function. Let
L<\omega
and
\bar{x}^{*}=\{x^{*}_{l}:l<L\}\subseteq\omega^{n}
such that all
x^{*}_{l}
are pairwise different.
Then,
Q\coloneqq\{p:\sigma^{p}=\sigma,s^{p}=s,n^{p}=n,\{x\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n:x\in F^{p}\}=\bar{x}^{*}\}

is centered and
D
-lim-linked for any ultrafilter
D
.

Proof.

We prove
(\star)_{k}
for
k\geq 1
in
5.24
.
We follow the proof of
5.33
, so we:

(1)

define a limit function
\lim^{D}\colon Q^{\omega}\to\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
,

(2)

take
X\in D
for a given
\bar{q}\in Q^{\omega}
and
r\leq\lim^{D}\bar{q}
, and

(3)

X
witnesses that
(\star)_{1}
is satisfied.

We will show
(\star)_{k}
for
k\geq 2
assuming
(\star)_{1}
.
Let
X(\bar{q},r)
denote the set
X\in D
constructed in (
2
) according to
\bar{q},r
.
Suppose that:

•

\bar{q}^{j}=\langle q_{m}^{j}:m<\omega\rangle\in Q^{\omega}
for
j<k
,

•

r\leq\lim^{D}\bar{q}^{j}
for all
j<k
,

By
(\star)_{1}
,
X^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcap_{j<k}X(\bar{q}^{j},r)\in D
.
For
m<\omega
and
j<k
, put
q^{j}_{m}=(\sigma,s,n,F^{j}_{m})
and
F^{j}_{m}=\{x^{m,j}_{l}:l<L\}
. For each
j<k
, let
B_{j}\subseteq L
and
n_{j,l}<\omega
for
l\in B_{j}
be
B
and
n_{l}
respectively in the proof of
5.33
by replacing
\bar{q}
with
\bar{q_{j}}
. For
j<k
and
l\in B_{l}
, let
X^{j,l}_{4}\coloneqq\{m<\omega:x^{m,j}_{l}(n_{j,l})\geq k\cdot L\}\in D
. Put
X_{4}\coloneqq\bigcap\{X^{j,l}_{4}:j<k,l\in B_{l}\}\in D
.
Put
X\coloneqq X^{\prime}\cap X_{4}
.
To see that
X
witnesses
(\star)_{k}
is true,
let
m\in X
, and we will find
q^{\prime}
as a common extension of
r
and all
q_{m}^{j}
. Put
r=(\sigma^{r},s^{r},n^{r},F^{r})
and define
q^{\prime}\coloneqq(\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime},n^{r},F^{r}\cup\bigcup_{j<k}F^{j}_{m})
as follows:

\sigma^{\prime}\supseteq\sigma^{r}
and for all
(u,v)\in(\omega\times\omega)^{<\omega}\setminus\dom(\sigma^{r})
of the form
(x^{m,j}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i,x^{m,j}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
for some
j<k
,
l,l^{\prime}<L
,
i<n_{r}
, let
(u,v)\in\dom(\sigma^{\prime})
and:

(5.17)

\sigma^{\prime}(u,v)\coloneqq\max\{x^{m,j^{*}}_{l^{*}}(i):j^{*}<k,l^{*}<L\}.

s^{\prime}\supseteq s^{r}
and for all
(u^{\prime},v)\in\omega^{<\omega}\times\omega^{<\omega}\setminus\dom(s^{r})
of the form
(x^{m,j}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{j,l}+1),x^{m,j}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{j,l})
for some
j<k
,
l\in B_{j}
and
l^{\prime}<L
, let
(u^{\prime},v)\in\dom(s^{\prime})
and:

(5.18)

s^{\prime}(u^{\prime},v)\coloneqq\bigcup\{x^{m,j^{*}}_{l^{*}}(n_{j,l}):j^{*}<k,l^{*}<L\}.

For such
(u^{\prime},v)=(x^{m,j}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{j,l}+1),x^{m,j}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{j,l})
, by the choice of
X_{4}
, we have
x^{m,j}_{l}(n_{j,l})\geq k\cdot L\geq|s^{\prime}(u^{\prime},v)|
and hence (
5.18
) is a valid definition of
s^{\prime}
.

Then, the argument of the case
(\star)_{1}
in the proof of
5.33
implies that
q^{\prime}
extends
r
and all
q^{j}_{m}
. The only difference appears in the final case of that proof, in which case we have
x^{m,j}_{l^{\prime}}(i)\leq s^{\prime}(x^{m,j}_{l}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(i+1),x^{m,j}_{l^{\prime}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}i)
by our current choice of
s^{\prime}
and hence
q^{\prime}\leq q^{j}_{m}
by the definition of
\leq_{\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}}
.
∎

Remark 5.47
.

In the previous argument, when we define
s^{\prime}(u^{\prime},v)
in (
5.18
), even in the case that
(x^{m,j}_{l_{0}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{j,l}+1),x^{m,j}_{l_{1}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{j,l})=(x^{m,j^{*}}_{l_{2}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}(n_{j^{*},l}+1),x^{m,j^{*}}_{l_{3}}\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}n_{j^{*},l})
for some
j,j^{*}<k
,
l_{0}\in B_{j}
,
l_{2}\in B_{j^{*}}
and
l_{1},l_{3}<L
, it could be the case that
x^{m,j}_{l_{1}}(n_{j,l})
and
x^{m,j^{*}}_{l_{3}}(n_{j^{*},l})
are different. For this reason, we are not sure if
(\star)_{k}
for
k\geq 2
is true for
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}}
.

5.3.2.
The
f
-linear growth ideal
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
and the poset
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
.

We will now modify the forcing
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
so that it will have closed-UF-limits. We introduce the
f
-linear growth ideal

\mathscr{I}^{f}_{L}
as well as its associated poset to increase
\non(K_{\mathscr{I}^{f}_{L}})
.

Definition 5.48
.

For
g,h\in{\omega}^{\omega}
, we write

g\ll h
if
\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{g(n)}{h(n)}=0
. Let
\exp\in{\omega}^{\omega}
denote the exponential function, i.e.
\exp(n)=2^{n}
.

Definition 5.49
.

Fix an interval partition
\bar{P}^{\mathrm{exp}}=(P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}})_{i<\omega}
of
\omega
such that
|P^{\mathrm{exp}}_{i}|=\exp(i)
. Let
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
be a function such that
f\ll\exp
.
The
f
-linear growth ideal

\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
is given by:

\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\coloneqq\left\{{A\subseteq\omega}:{\exists k<\omega~\forall^{\infty}i<\omega~\left|A\cap P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}}\right|\leq k\cdot f(i)}\right\}.

In particular, when
f
is the identity function,
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
is the linear growth ideal (cf.
[
Hru11
, Page 56]
,
[
BM14
, Page 3]
), from which the subscript
L
comes.
Note that
f\leq^{*}g
implies
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\subseteq\mathscr{I}_{L}^{g}
and if
f
is bounded then
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\cong_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
. Moreover, if
f\ll\exp
then
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathcal{Z}
; this follows almost directly from the definition.

Fix
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
with
f\ll\exp
.
We introduce the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
which generically adds a
\mathbf{K}_{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
-dominating function
\phi_{G}\colon\omega\to\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
.
We may assume that
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
is defined on
(P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}})_{i<\omega}
, instead of the interval partition
(P_{i})_{i<\omega}
with
|P_{i}|=i
. Under this assumption, the conditions of
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
are exactly the same as
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
but the order is weaker. Let
P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}}\coloneqq I_{i}\coloneqq\left[M_{i},M_{i+1}\right)
for each
i<\omega
.

Definition 5.50
.

The poset
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
is defined as follows:

•

Conditions are
p=(i,k,s,\varphi)=(i_{p},k_{p},s_{p},\varphi_{p})
where
i,k<\omega
,
s\in([M_{i}]^{\leq k})^{<\omega}
and
\varphi\colon\omega\to[\omega]^{\leq k}
such that
\varphi\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}|s|=s
.

•

The order
(i^{\prime},k^{\prime},s^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})\leq(i,k,s,\varphi)
is defined by:
i^{\prime}\geq i
,
k^{\prime}\geq k
,
\varphi^{\prime}(n)\supseteq\varphi(n)
for all
n<\omega
,
|s^{\prime}|\geq|s|
and for
n<|s|
,

(5.19)

\displaystyle s^{\prime}(n)\cap M_{i}=s(n),\text{ and}

(5.20)

\displaystyle\text{if }i\leq j<i^{\prime},\text{ then }|s^{\prime}(n)\cap I_{j}|\leq n\cdot f(j).

•

For a generic filter
G
, define
\phi_{G}\colon\omega\to\mathcal{P}(\omega)
by:

\phi_{G}(n)\coloneq\bigcup\{s_{p}(n):p\in G\text{ and }n<|s|\}.

One can easily prove:

Lemma 5.51
.

The following sets are open dense:

(1)

For
x\in{\omega}^{\omega}
,
\{p:x(n)\in\varphi_{p}(n)\text{ for all }n\geq|s_{p}|\}
,

(2)

for
i<\omega
,
\{p:i_{p}\geq i\}
,

(3)

for
n<\omega
,
\{p:|s_{p}|\geq n\}
.

By (
5.20
) and by Lemma
5.51
(
1
), we have:

Lemma 5.52
.

In
V[G]
,
\ran(\phi_{G})\subseteq\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
and
{\omega}^{\omega}\cap V\subseteq K_{\phi_{G}}
.

Definition 5.53
.

For
i,k<\omega
and
s\in([M_{i}]^{\leq k})^{<\omega}
,
define
Q=Q_{i,k,s}\coloneq\{p\in\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}:i_{p}=i,k_{p}=k,s_{p}=s\}
.

Lemma 5.54
.

Q
is centered. In particular,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
is
\sigma
-centered.

When
f
is unbounded,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
has closed-UF-limits.

Lemma 5.55
.

Assume
f\gg 1
. Let
i,k<\omega
,
s\in([M_{i}]^{\leq k})^{<\omega}
with
|s|\geq 1
and
D
be an ultrafilter. Then,
Q=Q_{i,k,s}
is closed-
D
-lim-linked. In particular,
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
has closed-UF-limits.

Proof.

The argument is based on the proof of
5.39
.
Under the assumption that
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
is defined on
(P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}})_{i<\omega}
, we make use of the same limit function
\lim^{D}
as in the proof of
5.39
.
Let
0<L<\omega
, we will show
(\star)_{L}
as required in Lemma
5.24
: Suppose that
\bar{q}^{l}=\langle(i,k,s,\varphi_{m}^{l})\rangle_{m<\omega}\in Q^{\omega}
and
q_{\infty}^{l}=\lim^{D}\bar{q}_{l}=(i,k,s,\varphi_{\infty}^{l})
for
l<L
and
q^{\prime}=(i^{\prime},k^{\prime},s^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})\leq q_{\infty}^{l}
for all
l<L
.
As
f\gg 1
, we may assume that:

(5.21)

kL\leq|s|\cdot f(j)\text{ for any }j\geq i^{\prime},

by increasing
i^{\prime}
.
Take
X\in D
such that:

(5.22)

\text{For all }m\in X,n\in|s^{\prime}|\setminus|s|\text{ and }l<L,~\varphi_{m}^{l}(n)\cap M_{i^{\prime}}=\varphi_{\infty}^{l}(n).

Fix
m\in X
and we will define a common extension
r
of
q^{\prime}
and all
q^{l}_{m}
for
l<L
. Define
\psi\colon\omega\to[\omega]^{\leq k^{\prime}+kL}
by
\psi(n)\coloneq\varphi^{\prime}(n)\cup\bigcup_{l<L}\varphi^{l}_{m}(n)
and
t\coloneq\psi\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}|s^{\prime}|
. Put
r\coloneq(i^{\prime\prime},k^{\prime}+kL,t,\psi)
, where
i^{\prime\prime}\geq i^{\prime}
large enough so
r
is a valid condition.
Then, the argument of the proof of
5.39
implies that
r\leq q^{\prime},q^{l}_{m}
for
l<L
.
The only difference appears when, in order to see
r\leq q^{\prime}
, we prove the following:

\text{ for }n\in|t|\setminus|s|,\text{ if }i^{\prime}\leq j\leq i^{\prime\prime},\text{then }|t(n)\cap I_{j}|\leq n\cdot f(j).

It follows from
|t(n)\cap I_{j}|\leq|\bigcup_{l<L}\varphi^{l}_{m}(n)|\leq kL\leq|s|\cdot f(j)\leq n\cdot f(j)
by (
5.21
).
∎

Remark 5.56
.

When proving
(\star)_{L}
for
L>1
, it seems that
f\gg 1
is necessary to have (
5.21
). Because of this, we are not sure if the assumption
f\gg 1
can be dropped.
For this reason, we are not sure if
(\star)_{k}
for
k\geq 2
is true for
\mathbb{P}^{\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}}
.

5.3.3.
Extended Cichoń’s maximum

Now we are ready to construct a model of extended Cichoń’s maximum. As explained in the beginning of this section, the forcing construction consists of two steps: the first one is to separate the left side of the diagram (
5.60
,
Figure

4
) with some additional properties (
\mathbf{R}_{i}\cong_{T}[\lambda_{7}]^{<\lambda_{i}}
in
5.60
) and the second one is to separate the right side (
5.61
,
Figure

5
) using these properties. The framework of the forcing construction is based on
[
Yam25
]
,
[
Yam24
]
(as well as the notation). In this paper, we do not describe the framework in detail (see
[
Yam25
]
,
[
Yam24
]
for the details), but we list necessary items to state and prove
5.60
,
5.61
.

The locally eventually different forcing
\mathbb{LE}
was introduced in
[
Yam24
]
to increase
\non(\mathcal{M})
, while keeping
\non(\mathcal{E})
small.

Definition 5.57
.

The poset
\mathbb{LE}
is defined as follows:

(1)

The conditions are triples
(d,s,\varphi)
where
d\in 2^{<\omega}
,
s\in\prod_{n<|d|}\exp(n)
and
\varphi\in\prod_{n<\omega}\mathcal{P}(\exp(n))
such that, for some
k<\omega
:

\dfrac{|\varphi(n)|}{\exp(n)}\leq\exp\left(-\dfrac{n}{2^{k}}\right)\text{ for all }n\geq|d|.

(2)

(d^{\prime},s^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})\leq(d,s,\varphi)
if
d^{\prime}\supseteq d
,
s^{\prime}\supseteq s,\varphi^{\prime}(n)\supseteq\varphi(n)
for
n<\omega
and:

\text{for all }n\in(d^{\prime})^{-1}(\{1\})\setminus d^{-1}(\{1\}),s^{\prime}(n)\notin\varphi(n).

Lemma 5.58

(
[
Yam24
, Lemma 4.27,4.29]
)
.

\mathbb{LE}
is
\sigma
-centered and
\sigma
-closed-UF-lim-linked (witnessed by the same countable components).

Definition 5.59
.

Put the following relational systems and forcing notions:

•

\mathbf{R}_{1}\coloneq\mathcal{N}
and
\mathbb{R}_{1}\coloneq\mathbb{A}
, the Amoeba forcing.

•

\mathbf{R}_{2}\coloneq C_{\mathcal{N}}^{\bot}
and
\mathbb{R}_{2}\coloneq\mathbb{B}
, the random forcing.

•

\mathbf{R}_{3}\coloneq\mathbf{D}
and
\mathbb{R}_{3}\coloneq\mathbb{D}
, the Hechler forcing.

•

\mathbf{R}_{4}\coloneq\mathbf{M}_{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
,
\mathbf{R}_{4}^{*}\coloneq\mathbf{M}_{\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}}
and
\mathbb{R}_{4}\coloneq\mathbb{\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}}
.

•

\mathbf{R}_{5}\coloneq C_{\mathcal{E}}
,
\mathbf{R}_{5}^{\mathcal{Z}}\coloneqq\mathbf{M}_{\mathcal{Z}}
,
\mathbf{R}_{5}^{f}\coloneq\mathbf{K}_{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
, and
\mathbb{R}_{5}^{f}\coloneq\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
for
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
with
1\ll f\ll\exp
.

•

\mathbf{R}_{6}\coloneq C_{\mathcal{M}}
, and
\mathbb{R}_{6}\coloneq\mathbb{LE}
.

Let
I^{+}\coloneq\{1,2,\ldots,6\}
be the index set.

Theorem 5.60
.

Assume:

•

\lambda_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\lambda_{6}
are uncountable regular cardinals and
\lambda_{7}\geq\lambda_{6}
is a cardinal.

•

\lambda_{3}=\mu_{3}^{+}
,
\lambda_{4}=\mu_{4}^{+}
and
\lambda_{5}=\mu_{5}^{+}
are successor cardinals and
\mu_{3}
is regular.

•

\kappa<\lambda_{i}
implies
\kappa^{\aleph_{0}}<\lambda_{i}
for all
i\in I^{+}
.

•

\lambda_{7}^{<\lambda_{6}}=\lambda_{7}
, hence
\lambda_{7}^{<\lambda_{i}}=\lambda_{7}
for all
i\in I^{+}
.

•

\lambda_{7}\leq 2^{\mu_{3}}
.

Then, there is a ccc poset which forces that for each
i\in I^{+}
,
\mathbf{R}_{i}\cong_{T}C_{[\lambda_{7}]^{<\lambda_{i}}}\cong[\lambda_{7}]^{<\lambda_{i}}
, in particular,
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{R}_{i})=\lambda_{i}
and
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{R}_{i})=\mathfrak{c}=\lambda_{7}
(see
Figure

4
, and the same also holds for
\mathbf{R}_{4}^{*}
,
\mathbf{R}_{5}^{\mathcal{Z}}
and
\mathbf{R}_{5}^{f}
for
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
with
1\ll f\ll\exp
).

Proof.

The following construction of the forcing iteration is based on
[
Yam24
]
and it is obtained by replacing
\mathbb{PR}
with
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{J}_{L}}
and
\mathbb{PR}_{g}
with
\mathbb{P}^{\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}}
in
[
Yam24
, Construction 5.6]
. More specifically, perform a finite support iteration
\langle\mathbb{P}_{\alpha},\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\xi}:\alpha\leq\gamma,\xi<\gamma\rangle
of length
\gamma\coloneqq\lambda_{7}+\lambda_{7}
such that for each
\xi<\lambda_{7}
,
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\xi}
is Cohen forcing and for each
\xi\in\gamma\setminus\lambda_{7}
,
\dot{\mathbb{Q}}_{\xi}
is a subforcing of
\mathbb{R}_{i}
of size
<\lambda_{i}
where
\xi\equiv i\in I^{+}
modulo
6
. By bookkeeping, we can have
\mathbf{R}_{i}\preceq_{T}C_{[\lambda_{7}]^{<\lambda_{i}}}
for
i\in I^{+}
(use
5.45
(3) for
i=4
and
5.52
for
i=5
). By
5.9
and
5.46
,
5.54
,
5.58
, we have
C_{[\lambda_{7}]^{<\lambda_{i}}}\preceq_{T}\mathbf{R}_{i}
for
i=1,2,6
. As in
[
Yam24
, Construction 5.6]
, we can inductively define names of ultrafilters for UF-limits and closed-UF-limits through the iteration construction, which are possible by
5.46
,
5.55
,
5.58
(and
5.54
).
C_{[\lambda_{7}]^{<\lambda_{i}}}\preceq_{T}\mathbf{R}_{i}
for
i=3,5
follows as in
[
Yam24
, Construction 5.6]
. By modifying the proof of
5.40
, we have
C_{[\lambda_{7}]^{<\lambda_{i}}}\preceq_{T}\mathbf{R}_{4}
(see also
[
Yam25
, Main Lemma 3.26]
).
∎

\add(\mathcal{N})

\cdot

. Note that
\non(M_{\mathcal{Z}})=\max\{\mathfrak{b},\non(\mathcal{E})\}
by
4.54
.

Theorem 5.61
.

Let
\aleph_{1}\leq\theta_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\theta_{12}
be regular cardinals and
\theta_{\mathfrak{c}}
an infinite cardinal with
\theta_{\mathfrak{c}}\geq\theta_{12}
and
\theta_{\mathfrak{c}}^{\aleph_{0}}=\theta_{\mathfrak{c}}
.
Then, there exists a ccc poset which forces
\mathfrak{b}(\mathbf{R}_{i})=\theta_{i}
and
\mathfrak{d}(\mathbf{R}_{i})=\theta_{13-i}
for each
i\in I^{+}
(the same also holds for
\mathbf{R}_{4}^{*}
,
\mathbf{R}_{5}^{\mathcal{Z}}
,
\mathbf{R}_{5}^{f}
for
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
with
1\ll f\ll\exp
) and
\mathfrak{c}=\theta_{\mathfrak{c}}
(see
Figure

5
).
In particular, in the forcing extension, for ideals
\mathscr{I}_{0},\mathscr{I}_{1}
on countable sets such that
\mathscr{J}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}_{0}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
and
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}_{1}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathcal{Z}
for some
1\ll f\ll\exp
, we have:

\non(M_{\mathscr{I}_{0}})=\theta_{4},~\cov(M_{\mathscr{I}_{0}})=\theta_{9},

\non(K_{\mathscr{I}_{1}})=\non(M_{\mathscr{I}_{1}})=\theta_{5},~\cov(K_{\mathscr{I}_{1}})=\cov(M_{\mathscr{I}_{1}})=\theta_{8}.

Proof.

See
[
GKMS22
]
(and
[
Yam24
, Theorem 5.9]
for the argument without
\mathsf{GCH}
).
∎
