.

6.
More on the ideals
\mathscr{J}_{L}
and
\mathscr{I}^{f}_{L}

In this final section, we collect several results related to
\mathscr{J}_{L}
and the
f
-linear growth ideals
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
introduced in
Section

5.3
. We start with considering the following variants of
\mathscr{J}_{L}
.

Definition 6.1
.

For any function
f\in\omega^{\omega}
with
f\gg 1
, an ideal
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}
on
\omega\times\omega
is defined by

\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}=\{A\subseteq\omega\times\omega:\exists k<\omega\,\forall^{\infty}i<\omega\,(\lvert(A)_{i}\rvert\leq k\cdot f(i))\}.

We simply write
\mathscr{J}_{L}
for
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}
in the case that
f
is the identity function.

These ideals appeared in
[
DFGT21
]
. According to their notation,
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}=I(\mathcal{F}_{f})
. By definition,
\mathcal{ED}\subseteq\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\subseteq\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
for any
f\in\omega^{\omega}
with
f\gg 1
. Also, note that if
f\ll\exp
then
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\upharpoonright\Delta_{\mathrm{exp}}
, where
\Delta_{\mathrm{exp}}=\{\langle i,j\rangle:j\leq\exp(i)\}
, is isomorphic to
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
.

Now recall that
\mathcal{ED}
is a critical ideal for local selectivity in the following sense.

Definition 6.2

(
[
BTW82
]
)
.

Let
\bar{P}=(P_{i})_{i<\omega}
be a partition of
\omega
. We say that
A\subseteq\omega
is a
selector of
\bar{P}
if
|A\cap P_{i}|\leq 1
for all
i<\omega
. An ideal
\mathscr{I}
on
\omega
is called
locally selective
if for every partition
\bar{P}
of
\omega
into sets in
\mathscr{I}
, there is an
\mathscr{I}
-positive selector of
\bar{P}
.

Fact 6.3

(
[
Hru11
, Proposition 3.2]
)
.

For any ideal
\mathscr{I}
on
\omega
,
\mathcal{ED}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}
if and only if
\mathscr{I}
is not locally selective.

Analogously,
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}
is critical for “locally linear selectivity.”

Definition 6.4
.

Let
\bar{P}=(P_{i})_{i<\omega}
be a partition of
\omega
. We say that
A\subseteq\omega
is a
linear selector
of
\bar{P}
if
|A\cap P_{i}|\leq i
for all
i<\omega
. An ideal
\mathscr{I}
on
\omega
is
locally linear selective
if for every partition
\bar{P}
of
\omega
into sets in
\mathscr{I}
, there is an
\mathscr{I}
-positive linear selector of
\bar{P}
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be an ideal on
\omega
. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}
for all
f\in\omega^{\omega}
such that
f\gg 1
.

(2)

\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}
for some
f\in\omega^{\omega}
such that
f\gg 1
.

(3)

\mathscr{I}
is not locally linear selective.

Proof.

(1) trivially implies (2). To show that (2) implies (3), assume that
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}
is witnessed by
\pi\colon\omega\to\omega\times\omega
. Let

P_{i}=\bigcup\{\pi^{-1}[\{j\}\times\omega]:f(j)=i\}.

Then
\overline{P}=\langle P_{i}\rangle_{i<\omega}
is a partition of
\omega
into sets in
\mathscr{I}
, since each
\{j\}\times\omega
belongs to
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}
and for each
i
there are only finitely many
j
such that
f(j)=i
by
f\gg 1
. Now let
A\subseteq\omega
be a linear selector of
\overline{P}
. Then for all
i<\omega
,

\lvert(\pi[A])_{i}\rvert\leq\lvert\pi[A\cap P_{f(i)}]\rvert\leq\lvert A\cap P_{f(i)}\rvert\leq f(i)

and thus
\pi[A]\in\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}
. So
A\subseteq\pi^{-1}[\pi[A]]\in\mathscr{I}
.

To show that (3) implies (1), let
f\in\omega^{\omega}
be such that
f\gg 1
and let
\overline{P}=\langle P_{i}\rangle_{i<\omega}
be a partition of
\omega
into sets in
\mathscr{I}
such that all linear selectors are in
\mathscr{I}
. Take any function
\pi\colon\omega\to\omega\times\omega
such that for all
i<\omega
,
\pi[P_{i}]\subseteq\{j_{i}\}\times\omega
and
\pi\mathpunct{\upharpoonright}P_{i}
is injective, where
j_{i}
is the least
j_{i}
such that
f(j_{i}+1)>i
.
To show that
\pi
witnesses
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}
, let
A\in\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}

and take
k,j^{*}<\omega
such that for all
j\geq j^{*}
,

\lvert(A)_{j}\rvert\leq k\cdot f(j)
.
Let
i_{0}<\omega
be such that
j_{i}\geq j^{*}
for all
i\geq i_{0}
. Then, for all
i\geq i_{0}
,

\lvert\pi^{-1}[A]\cap P_{i}\rvert=\lvert\pi[\pi^{-1}[A]\cap P_{i}]\rvert\leq\lvert(A)_{j_{i}}\rvert\leq k\cdot f(j_{i})\leq k\cdot i.

Thus
\pi^{-1}[A]
is included by a union of
(P_{i})_{i<i_{0}}
and
k
-many linear selectors of
\bar{P}
, so
\pi^{-1}[A]\in\mathscr{I}
by (3).
∎

In particular,
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}
does not depend on
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
in the sense of Katětov–Blass order:

Corollary 6.6
.

\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\cong_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{J}_{L}
for any
f\gg 1
.

Proof.

In the previous proof of (3)
\Rightarrow
(1), when
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
is the identity function,
j_{i}=i
and hence
\pi\colon\omega\to\omega\times\omega
is injective. This implies that if
\mathscr{I}
is not locally linear selective, then
\mathscr{J}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}
. Thus
\mathscr{J}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}
for any
f\gg 1
by (2)
\Rightarrow
(3).
\mathscr{J}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{J}_{L}
follows from (2)
\Rightarrow
(1).
∎

The relation of
\mathscr{J}_{L}
and other Borel ideals with respect to the Katětov order can be summarized as follows.

Proposition 6.7
.

(1)

\mathscr{J}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
for all
1\ll f\ll\exp
.

(2)

\mathcal{ED}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{J}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
and
\mathcal{ED}\not\geq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{J}_{L}\not\geq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
.

(3)

\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{J}_{L}
and
\mathscr{J}_{L}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
.

Proof.

(1)
\mathscr{J}_{L}\cong_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{J}^{f}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}^{f}_{L}
follows from
6.6
.

(2)
\mathcal{ED}\subseteq\mathscr{J}_{L}\subseteq\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
by definition, so
\mathcal{ED}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{J}_{L}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
hold. To see
\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{J}_{L}
, note that any tall analytic P-ideal is Katětov–Blass above some
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
, and thus above
\mathscr{J}_{L}
. Since
\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{I}
for any P-ideal
\mathscr{I}
(cf.
[
BF17
, Observation 2.3]
),
\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{J}_{L}
. To show that
\mathscr{J}_{L}\not\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathcal{ED}
, it is enough to show that
\mathcal{ED}
does not satisfy (3) in
6.5
. Let
\langle P_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
be a partition of
\omega
into sets in
\mathcal{ED}
. There are two cases:

(i)

For infinitely many
i<\omega
, there is
n<\omega
such that
\lvert P_{n}\cap(\{i\}\times\omega)\rvert=\omega
.

(ii)

For all but finitely many
i<\omega
, there are infinitely many
n<\omega
such that
P_{n}\cap(\{i\}\times\omega)\neq\emptyset
.

In both cases, it is easy to find a linear-selector of
\langle P_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
that is not in
\mathcal{ED}
.

(3) Since
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
(cf.
[
Hru17
]
),
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathscr{J}_{L}
. To show that
\mathscr{J}_{L}\not\leq_{\mathrm{K}}\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
, it is enough to show that
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
does not satisfy (3) in
6.5
. Let
\langle P_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
be a partition of
\omega
into sets in
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
. Let
\Delta_{n}=\{n\}\times(n+1)
. Then either

(i)

\sup_{n<\omega}\lvert\{k<\omega:P_{k}\cap\Delta_{n}\neq\emptyset\}\rvert<\omega
, or

(ii)

\sup_{n<\omega}\lvert\{k<\omega:P_{k}\cap\Delta_{n}\neq\emptyset\}\rvert=\omega
.

In either case, it is easy to find a linear-selector of
\langle P_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
that is not in
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

Since
\mathscr{J}_{L}
is not a P-ideal and not Katětov above
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}
,

\add^{*}(\mathscr{J}_{L})=\non^{*}(\mathscr{J}_{L})=\omega.

It is easy to see that
\mathscr{J}_{L}
has an uncountable strongly unbounded set, so we have

\displaystyle\add^{*}_{\omega}(\mathscr{J}_{L})=\add(M_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})=\add(K_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})=\omega_{1},

\displaystyle\cof^{*}(\mathscr{J}_{L})=\cof^{*}_{\omega}(\mathscr{J}_{L})=\cof(M_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})=\cof(K_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})=\mathfrak{c}.

In
[
DFGT21
]
,
\cov^{*}(\mathscr{J}_{L})=\non(\mathcal{M})
is shown. It is not hard to show
\non^{*}_{\omega}(\mathscr{J}_{L})=\cov(\mathcal{M})
; since
\mathcal{ED}\subseteq\mathscr{J}_{L}
,
\cov(\mathcal{M})=\non^{*}_{\omega}(\mathcal{ED})\leq\non^{*}_{\omega}(\mathscr{J}_{L})
holds and the proof of
\non^{*}_{\omega}(\mathcal{ED})\leq\cov(\mathcal{M})
(
3.34
) can be modified to show that
\non^{*}_{\omega}(\mathscr{J}_{L})\leq\cov(\mathcal{M})
.
Since
\mathcal{ED}\subseteq\mathscr{J}_{L}\subseteq\mathrm{Fin}\otimes\mathrm{Fin}
, we have

\displaystyle\non(K_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})=\mathfrak{b},\cov(K_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})=\mathfrak{d},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}(2)\leq\non(M_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})\leq\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{const}}_{\leq}(2),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}_{\leq}(2)\leq\cov(M_{\mathscr{J}_{L}})\leq\mathfrak{v}^{\mathrm{const}}(2).

Next we consider the
f
-linear growth ideals
\mathscr{I}^{f}_{L}
. Unlike the
\mathscr{J}^{f}_{L}
’s, these ideals are not necessarily Katětov–Blass equivalent. Recall that
f\leq^{*}g
implies
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\subseteq\mathscr{I}_{L}^{g}
.

Proposition 6.9
.

There is no
\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}
-minimal or maximal member among the family
\{\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{L}}^{f}:1\ll f\ll\exp\}
.

Proof.

In the rest of the proof, we write
P_{n}=P_{n}^{\exp}
.
Let
F\coloneqq\{f\in{\omega}^{\omega}:1\ll f\ll\exp\}
.
For
f,g\in F
and
n<\omega
, define the following condition:

(6.1)

g(n)^{2}\leq f(n)\text{ and }g(n)^{2}\cdot\left(1+\sum_{k<n}\left\lceil\frac{2^{k}}{f(k)}\right\rceil\right)\leq 2^{n}.

It can be easily seen that “
\forall g\in F~\exists f\in F~\forall^{\infty}n<\omega~(\text{\eqref{eq:f_g_pigeon} holds})
” and “
\forall f\in F~\exists g\in F~\forall^{\infty}n<\omega~(\text{\eqref{eq:f_g_pigeon} holds})
”.
Thus, it suffices to show that if
f,g\in F
satisfy (
6.1
) for almost all
n<\omega
, then
\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{L}}^{g}\lneq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}_{\mathrm{L}}^{f}
.
Take such
f,g
and we may assume that (
6.1
) is true for all
n<\omega
.
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{g}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
is obvious as
g\leq^{*}f
. To see
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\not\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}_{L}^{g}
, suppose toward a contradiction that
\pi\colon\omega\to\omega
witnesses
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\leq_{KB}\mathscr{I}_{L}^{g}
. It suffices to find
A\in\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
such that
\pi^{-1}[A]\notin\mathscr{I}_{L}^{g}
.
To construct such a set
A
, we inductively define
n_{i}<\omega
and finite sets
A_{i}\subseteq\omega
as follows.
Let
i<\omega
and assume
(n_{j})_{j<i}
and
(A_{j})_{j<i}
are defined.
First, pick
n_{i}<\omega
so that if
A_{j}\cap P_{n}\neq\emptyset
for some
j<i
and
n<\omega
, then for all
m\geq n_{i}
,

(6.2)

\pi[P_{m}]\cap P_{n}=\emptyset.

This is possible because
\pi
is finite-to-one.
To define
A_{i}
,
put

N_{i}\coloneqq\sum_{k<n_{i}}\left\lceil\frac{2^{k}}{f(k)}\right\rceil,

and let
\langle I_{k}\rangle_{k<N_{i}}
be a subpartition of
\langle P_{n}\rangle_{n<n_{i}}
such that if
I_{k}\subseteq P_{n}
then
|I_{k}|\leq f(n)
. Put
I_{N_{i}}=\bigcup_{m\geq n_{i}}P_{m}
. The pigeonhole principle implies that there is
k\leq N_{i}
such that

\lvert\pi^{-1}[I_{k}]\cap P_{n_{i}}\rvert\geq g(n_{i})^{2},

since otherwise (
6.1
) implies

|P_{n_{i}}|=2^{n_{i}}<g(n_{i})^{2}\cdot(N_{i}+1)\leq 2^{n_{i}},

a contradiction.
Let
k_{i}
be the least such
k
. Let
\{a^{i}_{j}:j<l\}
be the increasing enumeration of
I_{k_{i}}\cap\pi[P_{n_{i}}]
and let
A_{i}=\{a^{i}_{j}:j<\min\{g(n_{i})^{2},l\}\}
.

Now set
A=\bigcup_{i<\omega}A_{i}
. Then the following hold:

(1)

For all
i<\omega
,
\lvert\pi^{-1}[A_{i}]\cap P_{n_{i}}\rvert\geq g(n_{i})^{2}
.

(2)

For all
i<\omega
,
\lvert A_{i}\rvert\leq g(n_{i})^{2}
and if
A_{i}\cap P_{n}\neq\emptyset
for some
n<n_{i}
then
\lvert A_{i}\rvert\leq\lvert I_{k_{i}}\rvert\leq f(n)
.

(3)

For all
n<\omega
, there is
i<\omega
such that
A\cap P_{n}=A_{i}\cap P_{n}
and such
i
is unique if
A\cap P_{n}\neq\emptyset
.

Note that the third clause follows from (
6.2
). Then
\pi^{-1}[A]\notin\mathscr{I}_{L}^{g}
because by (1),

\lvert\pi^{-1}[A]\cap P_{n_{i}}\rvert\geq\lvert\pi^{-1}[A_{i}]\cap P_{n_{i}}\rvert\geq g(n_{i})^{2}

for all
i<\omega
. To see
A\in\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
, it suffices to show that
\lvert A\cap P_{n}\rvert\leq f(n)
for all
n>0
. By (3), for each
n>0
, there is
i<\omega
such that
A\cap P_{n}=A_{i}\cap P_{n}
. Then by (2), if
n<n_{i}
, then
\lvert A_{i}\rvert\leq f(n)
, or else

\lvert A\cap P_{n}\rvert=\lvert A_{i}\cap P_{n}\rvert\leq g(n_{i})^{2}\leq f(n_{i})\leq f(n).

This completes the proof.
∎

Fact 6.10

(
[
HMTU17
, Theorem 2.2]
)
.

\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}
if and only if there is a partition of
\omega
into finite sets such that all the selectors are in
\mathscr{I}
.

Recall that an ultrafilter
\mathcal{U}
on
\omega
is
rapid
if for every partition
\bar{P}
of
\omega
into finite sets, there is a linear selector of
\bar{P}
in
\mathcal{U}
. According to the naming convention in
[
BTW82
]
, we introduce the following notion.

Definition 6.11
.

An ideal
\mathscr{I}
on
\omega
is
locally rapid
if for every partition
\bar{P}
of
\omega
into finite sets, there is an
\mathscr{I}
-positive linear selector of
\bar{P}
.

Proposition 6.12
.

The following are equivalent:

(1)

There is
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
such that
1\ll f\ll\exp
and
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}
.

(2)

\mathscr{I}
is not locally rapid.

Proof.

To show (
1
) implies (
2
), assume that
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
is such that
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}
, witnessed by the function
g\colon\omega\to\omega
. For
j\in\omega
, put
Q_{j}\coloneqq\bigcup\{P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}}:i<\omega,f(i)=j\}
and
P_{j}\coloneqq g^{-1}(Q_{j})
. Note that
\bar{Q}\coloneqq(Q_{j})_{j<\omega}
is a partition of
\omega
(possibly including
\emptyset
), so is
\bar{P}\coloneqq(P_{j})_{j<\omega}
. To see that
\bar{P}
witnesses (
2
), let
A\subseteq\omega
be a linear selector of
\bar{P}
.
Let
i<\omega
be arbitrary and
j=f(i)
.
Then,
g(A)\cap P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}}\subseteq g(A)\cap Q_{j}=g(A\cap g^{-1}(Q_{j}))=g(A\cap P_{j})
has size
\leq|A\cap P_{j}|\leq j=f(i)
since
A
is a linear selector of
\bar{P}
and we shall show
A\in\mathscr{I}
. Since
i
was arbitrary,
g(A)\in\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
(witnessed by
k=1
).
g
witnesses
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}
, so
A\subseteq g^{-1}(g(A))\in\mathscr{I}
.

To show (
2
) implies (
1
), let
\bar{P}=(P_{j})_{j<\omega}
be a partition of
\omega
into finite sets such that all the linear selectors are in
\mathscr{I}
. Take
i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<\omega
such that
|P_{j}|\leq|P_{i_{j}}^{\mathrm{exp}}|
and choose some function
f\in{\omega}^{\omega}
such that
f(i_{j})=j
for all
j
. We may assume
f\ll\exp
, by taking a subsequence of
(i_{j})_{j<\omega}
if necessary. Let
g\colon\omega\to\omega
be an injection such that
g(P_{j})\subseteq P^{\mathrm{exp}}_{i_{j}}
. To see that
g
witnesses
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}
, let us assume
A\in\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}
and we shall show
B\coloneqq g^{-1}(A)\in\mathscr{I}
. Take
k<\omega
such that for each
i<\omega
,
|A\cap P_{i}^{\mathrm{exp}}|\leq k\cdot f(i)
. For each
j<\omega
,
|B\cap P_{j}|=|g(B\cap P_{j})|\leq|A\cap P_{i_{j}}^{\mathrm{exp}}|\leq k\cdot f(i_{j})=k\cdot j
, so
B
can be partitioned into
k
-many sets
(B_{l})_{l<k}
such that for each
j<\omega
,
|B_{l}\cap P_{j}|\leq j
. By (
2
), each
B_{l}
is in
\mathscr{I}
and hence
B=\bigcup_{l<k}B_{l}\in\mathscr{I}
.
∎

Corollary 6.13
.

Let
\mathscr{U}
be an ultrafilter on
\omega
. Then
\mathscr{U}
is rapid if and only if
\mathscr{I}_{L}^{f}\not\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{U}^{*}
for all
f\in\omega^{\omega}
such that
1\ll f\ll\exp
.

For the following results, we will use Solecki’s characterization for analytic P-ideals
\mathscr{I}=\Exh(\phi)
in
2.4
. When
\mathscr{I}
is tall, the submeasure
\phi
has the following property:

Lemma 6.14

(
[
HH07
, Lemma 1.4]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{I}=\Exh(\phi)
where
\phi
is a lower semi-continuous submeasure on
\omega
. Then
\mathscr{I}
is tall if and only if
\lim_{n\to\omega}\phi(\{n\})=0
.

Proposition 6.15
.

Every tall analytic P-ideal is not locally rapid.

Proof.

Let
\mathscr{I}=\Exh(\phi)
where
\phi
is a lower semi-continuous submeasure as in
2.4
. Inductively take
M_{0}<M_{1}<\cdots<\omega
as follows:

M_{0}\coloneq 0
and for
i>0
,

M_{i}\coloneq\min\left\{M>M_{i-1}:\text{ for }n\geq M,~\phi(\{n\})\leq\dfrac{1}{i\cdot 2^{i}}\right\},

which is possible by Lemma
6.14
.
Let
P_{i}\coloneq\left[M_{i},M_{i+1}\right)
. To see the interval partition
\bar{P}=(P_{i})_{i<\omega}
witnesses (
2
) in
6.12
, assume
A\subseteq\omega
is a linear-selector of
\bar{P}
. For
i>i_{0}
:

\displaystyle\phi(A\cap P_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n\in A\cap P_{i}}\phi(\{n\})

\displaystyle\leq|A\cap P_{i}|\cdot\dfrac{1}{i\cdot 2^{i}}\leq\dfrac{1}{2^{i}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\phi(A\setminus M_{i})

\displaystyle=\phi\left(A\cap\bigcup_{k\geq i}P_{k}\right)=\sup_{i^{\prime}\geq i}\phi\left(A\cap\bigcup_{i\leq k\leq i^{\prime}}P_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i^{\prime}\geq i}\sum_{i\leq k\leq i^{\prime}}\phi\left(A\cap P_{k}\right)\leq\sum_{i^{\prime}\geq i}\dfrac{1}{2^{i^{\prime}}}=\dfrac{1}{2^{i-1}}.

Therefore,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi(A\setminus n)=0
, so
A\in\mathscr{I}=\Exh(\phi)
. Hence
\mathscr{I}
is not locally rapid.
∎

We end this section with providing a characterization of locally rapid Borel ideals. Such a characterization was suggested by Laflamme in
[
Laf96
, Section 4]
, but he did not write a precise statement in the paper, so we will include it with a proof for the record.
8
8

8

Note that an ideal is locally rapid if and only if its dual filter is weakly rapid in Laflamme’s sense.
Following
[
Laf96
]
and
[
KZ17
]
, we say that an ideal
\mathscr{I}
on
\omega
is
\omega
-diagonalizable by elements of
\mathcal{A}\subseteq[\omega]^{\omega}
if there is a sequence
\langle A_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
of elements of
\mathcal{A}
such that

\forall X\in\mathscr{I}~\exists n<\omega~\lvert A_{n}\cap X\rvert<\omega.

By definition,
\non^{*}(\mathscr{I})>\omega
if and only if
\mathscr{I}
is not
\omega
-diagonalizable by elements of
[\omega]^{\omega}
.
2.8
(2) states that if
\mathscr{I}
is Borel, these conditions are also equivalent to
\mathcal{ED}_{\mathrm{fin}}\leq_{\mathrm{KB}}\mathscr{I}
. We introduce the following variant of
\omega
-diagonalizability.

Definition 6.16
.

We say that
A\subseteq\Fin
is
unbounded
9
9

9

This terminology was used in
[
HMM10
]
. Unboundedness is also called
\mathrm{Fr}
-universality, where
\mathrm{Fr}
is the Fréchet filter, in
[
Laf96
]
.
if for any
k<\omega
, there is
a\in A
such that
a\cap k=\emptyset
. For an ideal
\mathscr{I}
on
\omega
and
\mathcal{A}\subseteq[\Fin]^{\omega}
, we say that
\mathscr{I}
is
\omega
-diagonalizable by unbounded elements of
\mathcal{A}
if there is a sequence
\langle A_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
of unbounded elements of
\mathcal{A}
such that

\forall X\in\mathscr{I}~\exists n<\omega~\lvert\{a\in A_{n}:a\subseteq X\}\rvert<\omega.

Next we introduce the locally rapid ideal game
\mathcal{G}_{\textrm{local-rapid}}(\mathscr{I})
according to Laflamme’s suggestion. The game consists of
\omega
rounds and a typical run of this game looks as follows.

\begin{array}[]{c|ccccc}\mathrm{I}&X_{0}&&X_{1}&&\cdots\\
\hline\cr\mathrm{II}&&s_{0}&&s_{1}&\cdots\end{array}

In this game, Player I must play finite subsets
X_{n}
of
\omega
and Player II must respond by choosing
s_{n}\in[\omega\setminus X_{n}]^{\leq n}
. Player I wins if and only if
\bigcup_{n<\omega}s_{n}\in\mathscr{I}
.

Proposition 6.17

(Laflamme
[
Laf96
]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be an ideal on
\omega
. Then the following holds.

(1)

Player I has a winning strategy in
\mathcal{G}_{\textrm{local-rapid}}(\mathscr{I})
if and only if
\mathscr{I}
is not locally rapid.

(2)

Player II has a winning strategy in
\mathcal{G}_{\textrm{local-rapid}}(\mathscr{I})
if and only if
\mathscr{I}
is
\omega
-diagonalizable by unbounded elements of
\bigcup_{n<\omega}[[\omega]^{\leq n}]^{\omega}
.

Borel determinacy immediately implies the desired characterization.

Corollary 6.18
.

For any Borel ideal
\mathscr{I}
on
\omega
,
\mathscr{I}
is locally rapid if and only if it is
\omega
-diagonalizable by unbounded elements of
\bigcup_{n<\omega}[[\omega]^{\leq n}]^{\omega}
.

In the proof of
6.17
, we use the following notation. For any strategy
\sigma
of Player I and any move
\overline{s}
of Player II,
\sigma*\overline{s}
denotes the next move of Player I according to
\sigma
against
\overline{s}
. Analogously, for any strategy
\tau
of Player II and any move
\overline{X}
of Player I,
\overline{X}*\tau
denotes the next move of Player II according to
\tau
against
\overline{X}
.

Proof of
6.17
.

To show (1), suppose that
\mathscr{I}
is not locally rapid witnessed by a partition
\langle P_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
of
\omega
into finite sets. We define a strategy
\sigma
for I by

\sigma*\langle s_{0},\ldots,s_{n-1}\rangle=\bigcup\{P_{k}:k<n\land P_{k}\cap\bigcup_{i<n}s_{i}\neq\emptyset\}

To show that
\sigma
is a winning strategy for Player I, suppose that II plays
\langle s_{i}\rangle_{i<\omega}
against
\sigma
. Let
S=\bigcup_{i<\omega}s_{i}
. Then for all
n<\omega
, there is
k\leq n
such that
S\cap P_{n}\subseteq s_{k}
and thus
\lvert S\cap P_{n}\rvert\leq k\leq n
. By the choice of
\langle P_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
,
S\in\mathscr{I}
. So Player I wins the game.

Now suppose that Player I has a winning strategy
\sigma
. Let
g\in\omega^{\omega}
such that
\sigma(\emptyset)\subseteq g(0)
and for each
n<\omega
,

\bigcup\{\sigma*\langle s_{0},\ldots,s_{n}\rangle:\forall i\leq n\,(s_{i}\subseteq g(n))\}\subseteq g(n+1).

Set
P_{0}=[0,g(1))
and
P_{n+1}=[g(n+1),g(n+2))
for all
n<\omega
. Suppose toward a contradiction that
\mathscr{I}
is locally rapid. Then there is
S\in\mathscr{I}^{+}
such that for all
n<\omega
,
\lvert S\cap P_{n}\rvert\leq n
. Take such an
S
and for each
i<2
, let
S_{i}=S\cap\bigcup_{k<\omega}P_{2k+i+1}
. Since
S=(S\cap P_{0})\cup S_{0}\cup S_{1}
, we have
S_{i}\in\mathscr{I}^{+}
for some
i<2
. Assuming
S_{0}\in\mathscr{I}^{+}
, Player II can defeat
\sigma
by playing
s_{2k}=\emptyset
and
s_{2k+1}=S_{0}\cap P_{2k+1}
for all
k<\omega
. Player II can defeat
\sigma
in an analogous way in the other case too, which is a contradiction.

To show (2), suppose that
\mathscr{I}
is
\omega
-diagonalized by a sequence
\langle A_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
of unbounded elements of
\bigcup_{n<\omega}[[\omega]^{\leq n}]^{\omega}
. Adding
[\omega]^{n}
into the sequence if necessary, we may assume that
A_{n}\subseteq[\omega]^{\leq n}
for all
n<\omega
. Fix a surjection
\pi\colon\omega\to\omega
such that
\pi(n)\leq n
and
\lvert\pi^{-1}(n)\rvert=\omega
for all
n<\omega
. Then we define a strategy
\tau
for Player II by

\langle X_{0},\ldots,X_{n}\rangle*\tau=\text{the lexicographically least element of }A_{\pi(n)}\cap[\omega\setminus(X_{n}\cup n)]^{\leq\pi(n)}.

This is well-defined since all
A_{n}
’s are unbounded. To show that
\tau
is a winning strategy for Player II, let
\langle s_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
be Player II’s move following
\tau
. Then the set
S:=\bigcup_{n<\omega}s_{n}
includes infinitely many members of each
A_{n}
. Since
\mathscr{I}
is
\omega
-diagonalized by
\langle A_{n}\rangle_{n<\omega}
,
S\in\mathscr{I}^{+}
.

Finally, suppose that Player II has a winning strategy
\tau
in the game. We define
X_{\overline{s}}
for any possible move
\overline{s}
of player II when she follows
\tau
. This is done by induction on the length of
\overline{s}
as follows: Let
X_{\emptyset}=\{\langle X\rangle*\tau:X\in\Fin\}
. For each
s\in X_{\emptyset}
, choose
X^{s}_{\emptyset}
such that
\langle X^{s}_{\emptyset}\rangle*\tau=s
. For any
\overline{s}
with
X_{\overline{s}}
well-defined and any
t\in X_{\vec{s}}
, define

X_{\overline{s}^{\frown}t}=\{\langle X_{\emptyset}^{s_{0}},\ldots,X_{\overline{s}}^{t},X\rangle*\tau:X\in\Fin\},

and for each
u\in X_{\overline{s}^{\frown}t}
, choose
X_{\overline{s}^{\frown}t}^{u}
such that
\langle X_{\emptyset}^{s_{0}},\ldots,X_{\overline{s}}^{t},X_{\overline{s}^{\frown}t}^{u}\rangle*\tau=u
. We claim that
\mathscr{I}
is
\omega
-diagonalized by the family of all the sets
X_{\overline{s}}
. Suppose otherwise. Then there is
Y\in\mathscr{I}
such that for any
\overline{s}
with
X_{\overline{s}}
well-defined, for infinitely many
t\in X_{\overline{s}}
,

\langle X_{\emptyset}^{s_{0}},\ldots,X_{\overline{s}}^{t}\rangle*\tau\subseteq Y.

Then one can easily produce a run that is a win for Player I, but Player II follows
\tau
. This is a contradiction.
∎
